8 Log-concavity, unimodality and positivity

64. Single curves do not force log-concavity
log-concavity-and-unimodality/cluster-variable-log-concavity-counterexample/article.pdf
from annular_single_lamination_counterexample.zip
An unpunctured annulus with one marked point on each boundary and a single unit-weight simple
lamination curve gives, after mutations 2, 1, 2 with the two mutable variables specialised to 1, the
polynomial 1 + 3q 2 + 2q 3 + 4q 4 + 2q 5 + q 6 ; the consecutive coefficients 3, 2, 4 form a strict valley,
refuting Conjecture 1 of arXiv:2508.04396v3 and conflicting with its general unpunctured-surface
unimodality statement. The geometric realisation is proved, not merely the matrix calculation,
and a fixed seed gives infinitely many such examples.
65. Cycles refute infinite log-concavity for chromatic coefficients
log-concavity-and-unimodality/infinite-log-concavity-of-cycles/article.pdf
from chromatic_cycle_logconcavity.zip
For the cycle Cn with absolute chromatic coefficients under the iterated operator L(a)k =
a2k − ak−1 ak+1 : infinitely log-concave for 3 ≤ n ≤ 11; first failure at depth 5 for n = 12, depth 4 for
13 ≤ n ≤ 16, and depth 3 for n ≥ 17. So C12 is the smallest cycle counterexample (no minimum
over all graphs is claimed), with an explicit third-iterate witness polynomial valid for all n ≥ 3.
66. Oscillating Log-Concavity in Geometric Generating Functions
log-concavity-and-unimodality/oscillating-log-concavity-geometric-transform/article.pdf
from oscillating_log_concavity_research.zip
Challenges 2 and 3 in Section 3 of Heim and Neuhauser, arXiv:2302.13327v1, for the geometric
transform 1/(1 − uG(z)). For divisor-sum weights, every real exponent and every u > 0 give
infinitely many strict log-concavity and log-convexity indices with eventually bounded gaps;
likewise for integer power weights with exponent ≥ 2, the exponents 0 and 1 being explicitly
exceptional. For square weights at u = 1, every four consecutive indices carry both signs (sharp),
each with density 1/2 in every arithmetic progression. A subdominant-pole theorem is the common
mechanism.
67. A counterexample and a sharp spacing-three criterion for products of q-integers
log-concavity-and-unimodality/q-integer-product-unimodality/report.pdf
from q_unimodality_attack.zip
The necessity half of Conjecture 5.4 of Connelly, Ito, Martinez, Shevchenko and Yang
(arXiv:2605.12822v1) is false: (1 + q)6 (1 + q 3 ) is symmetric and weakly unimodal although no ai
is divisible by 3 and b = 2 exceeds the asserted bound, and the example lies inside the source’s
claimed range. The report also proves the proposed sufficient condition for every r ≥ 2, a necessary
degree bound in the absence of an r-divisible ordinary factor, and a complete classification of
spacings two and three.


                                                    26
68. Sharp size thresholds for failures of log-concavity in independence systems
log-concavity-and-unimodality/independence-system-thresholds/article.pdf
from sharp_log_concavity_thresholds.zip
For each rank r ≥ 4, determines the smallest ambient ground-set size admitting failure of ordinary,

systems, those satisfying Xie–Xu’s augmentation bound, and those with additive augmentation
parameter 3 (values 7/6/5, 7/7/6, 8/7/7, and r + 1 throughout for r ≥ 7). The initial target,
Xie–Xu Conjecture 4.8 (arXiv:2408.09152v1), had already been disproved by Alex Chengyu Li
in September 2026; the report states this and refines the target to the rankwise minimum-size
question.

70. A sum-of-squares proof of Ulas’s real-zero conjecture
log-concavity-and-unimodality/stern-polynomial-positivity/stern_conjecture_note.pdf

Conjecture 4.4 of Maciej Ulas, arXiv:1909.10844v1, for the Stern polynomials indexed by an =

was already in Ulas’s paper and is re-derived, not claimed as new.
71. Two-coin counterexamples to generalized Shepp–Olkin thresholds
log-concavity-and-unimodality/bernoulli-entropy-quasiconcavity/article.pdf
from bernoulli_entropy_research.zip; batch 43:   proveit_product_entropy.zip; batch 57:
ProveIt_Alphabet_Entropy.zip
For every finite real q > 1, both the Rényi-q and the Tsallis-q entropy of a sum of independent
Bernoulli variables fail even quasiconcavity as a function of the parameter vector, already on the
open square (0, 1)2 with two coins. This disproves the proposed thresholds — Rényi 2 and Tsallis
3.65986 . . . — adjoined to Conjecture 4.2 of arXiv:1503.01570 and restated in arXiv:1510.05390,
Section 8. Part II, added in batch 43, settles the subunit joint-concavity question for the joint
bit vector only: product Rényi entropy is concave in every dimension for 0 < q ≤ 2, and product
Tsallis entropy is concave for every 0 < q < 1 exactly when n ≤ 9. Part III, added in batch 57,
answers Part II’s question on larger alphabets for full categorical simplices: for 0 < q < 1 product
Tsallis entropy on ∆k1 × · · · × ∆kn is concave exactly when i Θki (q) ≤ 1 for an explicit alphabet
                                                             P

profile Θk ; at q = 1/2 three factors with a common alphabet of k symbols are concave exactly
when k ≤ 17; concavity for every choice of n alphabets holds exactly when nq ≤ 1; and for
1 < q < 2 concavity reduces to positive semidefiniteness of an n × n compression matrix. For the
ordinary sum Part I’s question stays open; an external claim about it is recorded as unverified.
72. Nonreal roots survive in polynomial iterative equations
log-concavity-and-unimodality/nonreal-roots-in-iterative-equations/article.pdf
from nonreal_roots_research.zip; batch 41: ProveIt_Increasing_Spectral_Core.zip; batch 42:
ProveIt_Orientation_Reversing_Spectra.zip; batch 54: ProveIt_Exact_Algebraic_Degree.zip
Here P [f ] = j aj f ◦j means a combination of iterates, not a polynomial in pointwise values.
              P

Gives a negative answer to Problem 6.1 of Draga–Morawiec (arXiv:1503.00570), which proposed
deleting nonreal maximal-modulus characteristic roots: an explicit odd, strictly increasing, globally
bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism realises a cubic whose nonreal root cannot be discarded. Adds a
classification of the circular root spectra that occur. Part II, added in batch 41, classifies the


                                                 27
minimal iterative polynomials of increasing homeomorphisms with roots on several circles, through
a universal spectral core and an affine rigidity theorem. Part III, added in batch 42, does
the same for decreasing homeomorphisms with P (0) ̸= 0: two-cycle and one-sided spectra, a
decreasing core with one or two witnesses, and a finite-smoothness classification. Part IV, added
in batch 54, answers Part III’s question on algebraic degree: for a polynomial coordinate H and
f = H ◦ (a id) ◦ H −1 the degree of f over R(x) is deg H/ deg W , where W is the largest normalized
right compositional factor of H with W (at) = adeg W W (t), and it is exactly deg H for every
coordinate of Part III’s finite-smoothness theorem. For every odd n ≥ 5 it gives real-analytic
involutions of algebraic degree n, with distortion arbitrarily close to 1, whose graph extension has
Galois group Sn , so they are not expressible by radicals.
73. Canonical binomial decomposition does not preserve real roots
log-concavity-and-unimodality/binomial-decomposition-real-roots/article.pdf
from mu_welker_recursive_decomposition.zip + recursive_binomial_counterexample.zip
A negative answer to Question 3.10 of Mu and Welker (arXiv:2503.24076v1) for their canonical
decomposition F (t) = G(t) + tH(t) built from the integer binomial expansion of every coefficient:
real-rootedness of F does not pass to the parts. Two cubic witnesses are carried, one with a
repeated root and one with simple roots, each with its own digit table, discriminant pair and exact
nonreal zeros; the report also gives the minimal witness within cubics, two all-degree thresholds
neither of which implies the other, and a two-sided limit. Merged; the two originals were two
write-ups of one proof program, each already containing both witnesses with the headline and the
co-star swapped.
74. A first-triple criterion for log-concavity of MDS weight distributions
log-concavity-and-unimodality/mds-weight-log-concavity/article.pdf
from mds_log_concavity_research.zip
For d = n − k + 1 ≥ 2, k ≥ 3 and q > d, the compressed formal MDS weight distribution
(1, Ad , . . . , An ) is log-concave if and only if the single inequality A2d+1 ≥ Ad Ad+2 holds — the
whole sequence is controlled by its first triple.
75. A uniform Hankel obstruction for higher-order Stirling subset polynomials
log-concavity-and-unimodality/stirling-hankel-obstruction/article.pdf
from stirling_hankel_obstruction.zip
Proves the higher-order negative assertion of Deb–Sokal Conjecture 1.4(d), with a stronger
statement at every positive shift: for r ≥ 3 and a ≥ 1 the 3 × 3 Hankel determinant det(sr,a+i+j (x))
of the row-generating polynomials fails the required positivity. Clause (d) of that conjecture;
clause (c) is the subject of the higher-order Stirling rows entry.

ProveIt_Rank_Six_Third_Newton_Inequality.zip (bundle Report 81)
Continues Parts XI–XIII of the preorder root-polytope report on whether the bipartite matching-
support polynomial pG , with positive vertex activities, is ultra-log-concave of the order of the
matching number ν(G); merged from twenty-seven manuscripts, one of them delivered only inside
another’s archive. Part I proves it for arbitrary positive activities on both shores whenever some
minimum vertex cover meets one shore in at most two vertices, through an explicit transversal-
matroid lift, so for every graph with ν(G) ≤ 5; with that report’s rank-six Hall counterexample
the least matching number of a weighted failure is exactly six. Equality in a normalized inequality
occurs only for balanced star forests, covers with two vertices on each shore give real-rooted
support polynomials, and the full Hall family is ultra-log-concave for all activities exactly when one
core has at most two vertices. Part II answers the unit-weight and one-shore versions negatively:
a connected all-unit graph with 6916 vertices and ν = 3450 fails, as does a 125-vertex graph
with ν = 50 weighted on one shore only, and for one common activity on the right core of a
full Hall graph the least failing matching number is exactly 38. Part III, at ν = 6 with all left
activities one, proves the second and last of the five inequalities for every graph (the first holds in
general), and all five for several core families. Part IV proves eventual ultra-log-concavity, after a
common scaling of the right part of a minimum cover, for every graph with ν(G) ≤ 8 and unit
left activities, and locates the least matching number of a persistent failure in [9, 38]. Results
that the preorder report already proves are cited there, not reprinted, and theorems proved by
several manuscripts are printed once with the other proofs as second routes. Part V, added in
batch 73 from four manuscripts written in parallel with batch 72, shows that at unit weights and
matching number three a nonreal zero needs fifteen vertices, attained by a unique graph; proves a
two-element Lorentzian deformation of every matroid basis polynomial and an all-rank first-layer
positive-semidefiniteness theorem (which proves Part III’s three-vertex marginal theorem without
its 5,339-monomial certificate); gives a stable seed for two-neighbour star cores; and shows by three
13-vertex examples that the two-tail lifts do not extend to three tails. What those manuscripts
re-prove of Parts I and III is cited or kept as second routes; six further archives of the batch (two
earlier editions, four re-proofs or special cases) were not placed. Part VI (batch 100), from one
further manuscript (thirty-two in all), proves that for a genuine 3 + 3 minimum cover with unit left
activities the quartic F4 = 15s4 + 10p1 s3 + 6p2 s2 + 3p3 s + p4 , with every right variable retained,
is Lorentzian, so the third inequality 9p23 ≥ 16p2 p4 holds for every graph of matching number six
with one weighted shore; with Parts I and III only the fourth, 8p24 ≥ 15p3 p5 , stays open there. The
proof is computer-assisted (exact class reductions, Rayleigh identities and a Bernstein certificate,
all in exact arithmetic). Also, with arbitrary positive left activities and every matching number


                                                  29
r, G ⪰ ddT /r on signed vectors, generalizing Part V’s first-layer theorem by the same route; a
rank-three 4 × 6 regular-minor matrix on which the corresponding matrix bound, normalized by
rank, fails on a signed vector (no positive-field failure is inferred); and nonnegativity of the cubic
core-restoration coefficients of the fourth gap. Its first-gap equality proof is a further second route
of the preorder report’s theorem. Retained-variable Lorentzianity for other cover orientations
is not claimed; the source’s full regeneration of its two largest certificate arrays was not rerun
here. The independent check (f8accf37c) found the write’s Remark 46.1 (a minimum cover with
exactly two right vertices also gives a Lorentzian F4 ) valid. Unrefereed; not formalized.

at the actual degree
log-concavity-and-unimodality/preorder-gamma-rank-ulc/article.pdf

Answers Research question 96 of the preorder root-polytope report: is the gamma polynomial
ΓD = k γk tk of a finite preorder, with γk counting ordered pairs of disjoint k-sets joined along
       P

arcs, ultra-log-concave after normalization by its actual degree? Part I proves it for every preorder
of actual degree at most four with unit activities: degree three by a Gallai–Edmonds reduction
and exact integer-population certificates (a nonreal-rooted cubic needs fifteen vertices), degree
four by bounded cores, 76 four-attachment templates and 50 global binomial-square identities,
with a smaller second route for the largest certificate. Part II treats independent tail and head
activities on arbitrary loopless directed relations: real stability of the signed monomer polynomial
under every two-by-two role cover (generalizing the 2 + 2 theorem of the matching-rank report),
coefficientwise Rayleigh inequalities for a three-vertex shore, rank ultra-log-concavity under every
physical vertex cover of size three and on bipartite physical graphs, a pendant-head stability
criterion, and the full statement for independently weighted preorders of actual degree at most
three. Part III adds a cloud of universal sinks to an arbitrary directed core: a sharp last-coefficient
bound for every core size, and the statement at every actual degree for four-vertex cores and at
actual degrees four and five for five-vertex cores. The degree-three and degree-four theorems are
computer-assisted. Merged from eleven manuscripts and a six-page supplement, from eighteen
archives (three of them handed over from the matching-support cluster); two earlier versions are
superseded and theorems proved more than once are printed once with second routes. Section 14
of Part III, added in batch 77 from a further manuscript, proves γ22 ≥ 3γ1 γ3 on the face γ4 = 0 for
every loopless directed five-vertex core with universal sinks and independent activities, with the
constant 3 sharp even for preorder cores, by a degree-drop reduction to a computer-assisted two-
sink lemma with 7,901 rational square certificates; so five-vertex cores are rank ultra-log-concave
at every actual degree. For preorder cores it is a case of Part II’s degree-three theorem, so the
new content is the non-transitive core; Section 12’s counterexample to the constant 3 has γ4 > 0.
Some certificate data over 2 MB, and Section 14’s 95.1 MB of certificate shards, which cannot be
regenerated, stay in the arrival commits, and the report says how to retrieve or rebuild them.


9 Quaternionic analysis
One report, first merged from ten manuscripts and since extended by two more. The subject is
new to this collection, and the delivery was unusual: one expository survey and nine independent
attempts at the same follow-up problem, all written on the same day. The nine agree, which is


                                                  30
the substance of the entry.
78. Slice regularity and the global inverse Fueter problem
quaternionic-analysis/slice-regularity-and-fueter-inversion/article.pdf
from quaternionic_analysis.zip and nine companion archives; batch 39:
ProveIt_Fueter_Polynomial_Periods.zip; batch 64: ProveIt_Fueter_Global_Inversion.zip
One article, first assembled from ten manuscripts. Part I is the delivered expository survey: slice
regularity and Cauchy–Fueter regularity side by side, with Cauchy formulas, Taylor and Laurent
expansions, the regular product and the geometry of zeros, the argument principle, Rouché, open
mappings, Schwarz–Pick and an intrinsic Riemann mapping theorem. Parts II–XI answer the
follow-up question the other nine manuscripts all attacked independently: for an axially monogenic
g = P +IQ, when is there a single-valued slice-regular f with ∆4 f = g? The answer is a two-period
obstruction. The closed one-forms ωg = 12 (−P dx + Q dy) and θg = 12 ((xP + yQ)dx + (yP − xQ)dy)
have vanishing periods exactly when a global primitive exists, and then f (x + Iy) = (x + Iy)C + V
with dC = ωg , dV = θg , unique modulo q 7→ qa + b; the monodromy is affine, and the cokernel of
the Fueter map has real dimension 8m. The point the nine exist to make is that θg is computed
from the target, with no first primitive chosen. Nine independent derivations reaching
the same two forms, the same 8m and the same logarithmic energy constant is the
evidence the entry rests on. Where they differed it was almost always notation — thirteen
conflicts are recorded and resolved in MERGE_NOTES.md — but two were mathematical and would
have produced a false statement if the merge had been mechanical: the size law at a critical
singularity has genuinely different sharp constants in two norms that four manuscripts both called
Mg , and the conserved flux recovering the slope residue needs the weight 3(Re q − u) + Im q
rather than q − u, the coefficient 3 being exactly what makes it right-Fueter-regular. All ten
source manuscripts and all ten verification programs are kept; the deduplication is in the article.
Parts XII–XIII, added in batch 39 from an eleventh manuscript, answer the report’s open problem
on higher dimensions in large part: in ambient dimension 2h + 2 one polynomial closed form of
degree 2h − 1 (at h = 1 the form θg + t ωg ) gives a complete obstruction, with a constructive
inverse, a cokernel of dimension 2h dV m, an exact residue filtration and sharp size and energy
constants. Part XIV, added in batch 64 from a twelfth manuscript, carries this across the real axis
and to planar domains of arbitrary, also infinite, connectivity: every admissible polynomial period
character is realized, a continuous linear right inverse of the period map exists exactly when the
admissible period rank is finite, and a continuous nonlinear section exists always.

79. Mutual-visibility sets in Andrásfai graphs
graph-theory/andrasfai-mutual-visibility/article.pdf

                                                                                       P

x(4 − 19x + 33x2 − 40x3 + 8x4 )/(1 − 10x + 29x2 − 32x3 + 8x4 ), by an exact weight-preserving

80. A bicyclic 30-vertex graph with domination root −4
graph-theory/domination-root-minus-four-order-30/domination_root_30.pdf
from domination_root_30_research.zip; batch 67:   ProveIt_Domination_Cycle_Obstructions.zip
An explicit connected planar graph on 30 vertices and 31 edges, treewidth 2 and domination
number 10, with D(G, x) = x10 (x + 4)Q19 (x) — a negative answer to question 2 of Section 4
in Alikhani and Griswold, arXiv:2608.00109v1, which asks whether 33 is the minimum order.

                                                  31
Their 33-vertex example had already disproved the original integer-root conjecture; no claim
of global minimality at 30 is made. Three independent exact polynomial computations agree.
Part II, added in batch 67, proves 1 ≤ |D(G, −1)| ≤ 3β sharply for cycle rank β, a simultaneous
divisibility budget for integer roots and their multiplicities, and the exact value sets of |D(G, −1)|:
{1, 3, 7, 9} for β ≤ 2, so no root −6 at cycle rank at most two, and a ten-element set for β ≤ 3
(computer-assisted). Part I’s zero-C four-cycle search forces |D(G, −1)| = 3, so it can produce no
integer root below −2 other than a simple −4; no root −6 or −8 is constructed.
81. A marked-cycle proof of the trapezohedral domination conjecture
graph-theory/trapezohedral-minimal-dominating-sets/article.pdf
from trapezohedral_domination_research.zip, A381190_proof_package.zip
Proves the generating function labelled conjectural in OEIS A381190: for the n-trapezohedral
graph, the number of dominating sets that are inclusion-minimal among all dominating sets and
induce a connected subgraph is an = 2n(2pn−2 + pn−3 ) with pm = pm−2 + pm−3 (Padovan). The
proof classifies such sets by a cyclic arrangement of gaps of length 2 and 3 with a restricted marked
edge. Adds the size distribution, strict log-concavity, an unbiased sampler, a Binet formula and a
central limit theorem.

84. Diagonal Hardinian Arrays: A Fixed-Parameter Asymptotic Formula
enumerative-combinatorics/hardinian-array-diagonal-asymptotics/article.pdf

90. Throwback Dynamics with Repeated Entries
enumerative-combinatorics/throwback-queue-dynamics/throwback_research.pdf
from throwback_research.zip
For the queue process that removes the head and reinserts it at the zero-based position equal to
its weight: every token recurs if and only if at most m input tokens have weight at most m, for
every m ≥ 1, with an explicit description of the recurrent tokens when that fails. Adds exact cycle
periods and frequencies, a coprimality criterion for perfect mixing, Catalan and prime-parking
counts for recurrent cores, and the largest labelled period of a core of size r.
91. Reciprocity and Matrix Duality for Preorder Polytopes
enumerative-combinatorics/preorder-polytope-reciprocity/article.pdf

Proves Conjectures 7.2, 4.2 and 4.4 of Athanasiadis and Chapoton, Polytopes and posets associated
to preorders (arXiv:2605.26916v1), and the ordinary q = 1 case of their Conjecture 4.9 (not its full
q-refinement). Two mechanisms: Ehrhart–Macdonald reciprocity after subtracting the all-ones
vector, extending the double identity to independently weighted capacities; and duplication of
both colour classes of the bipartite graph followed by Postnikov’s lattice-point formula, giving the
conjectured matrix transpose.
92. Support reciprocity and the q-zeta polynomial of preorder lattice-point posets
enumerative-combinatorics/preorder-q-zeta-reciprocity/article.pdf
from preorder_qzeta_reciprocity.zip + preorder_q_zeta_reciprocity.zip +

Proves the full q-refinement of Athanasiadis–Chapoton Conjecture 4.9, equation (21): for a

                                                  34
preorder τ on [n], with Pτ the nonnegative integer vectors satisfying a(I) ≤ |I| on every order ideal
and Bτ its maximal elements, Zq (Pτ , [−1]q ) = (−1)n b∈Bτ q −|supp(b)| , together with the stronger
                                                      P

identity at each exact support. The q = 1 case was already covered by the preorder-polytope
reciprocity report and is cited, not reclaimed. Merged from three reports that reached the same
two theorems by three architecturally independent routes — an incidence inversion with Boolean
localization, the same with a Hall-based privatization and a self-contained Euler identity, and a
capacity-enumerator reciprocity with a transfer involution — and all three survive.
93. An explicit shelling for preorder lattice-point posets
enumerative-combinatorics/preorder-shellability/article.pdf
from preorder_shellability_research.zip
An affirmative solution to Question 4.6 of Athanasiadis and Chapoton (arXiv:2605.26916v1, p. 13):
an explicit pure shelling of the order complex of Pτ with its minimum removed, and a coning that
shells the order complex of Pτ itself. Both complexes are therefore Cohen–Macaulay over the
integers and over every field. A different question of that paper from the three other preorder
entries.
94. Multiple-chain preorder polytopes
enumerative-combinatorics/multiple-chain-exponential-formula/article.pdf
from multiple_chain_exponential_formula_research.zip + multiple_chains_research.zip
Proves the exponential generating-function identity proposed in Section 9.5 of Athanasiadis and
Chapoton (arXiv:2605.26916v1) for lattice points of multiple-chain preorder polytopes counted
by number of nonzero coordinates, in a rectangular generalization with independent block width
and height. Adds a duality corollary, an Ehrhart evaluation, a degree-2d root theorem with a
quartic elimination certificate, two treatments of the γ-vector, a Stieltjes moment representation,
the boundary constant in two forms, and a central limit theorem with exact variance. Merged; the
identity is proved twice, once through a subset encoding with a bounded symbol and once through
slack meanders in a Laurent ring, and the eleven deliberate redundancies are listed in the text.
95. Rationality and growth of stabilized permutation-weight series
enumerative-combinatorics/stabilized-permutation-weights/article.pdf
from stabilized_permutation_weights.zip
Studies the full stabilized generating functions of the permutation-weight q-Eulerian polynomials,
beyond their initial connection with OEIS A256193: an all-d finite-spectrum theorem, rationality,
a universal integer denominator, the exact radius of convergence and residue-class asymptotics.
The two-descent sequence gets an explicit formula in powers of 3, powers of 2 and Fibonacci
numbers.
96. A Reflexive Root-Polytope Model for Preorder h-Polynomials
enumerative-combinatorics/preorder-root-polytopes/article.pdf
from preorder_root_polytopes.zip; batch 39: ProveIt_Height_Two_Preorder_Research.zip; batch 42:
ProveIt_Cactus_Rigidity.zip; batch 44: ProveIt_Preorder_Gamma.zip,
ProveIt_Preorder_Gamma_Research.zip; batch 55: ProveIt_Lorentzian_Support.zip,
ProveIt_Support_Polynomials_Research.zip, ProveIt_ADE_Stability.zip, ProveIt_Matroid_Lifts.zip;
batch 63: ProveIt_Marked_Tree_Stability.zip; batch 64: ProveIt_Two_Flow_Lattice_Sampling.zip;
batch 66: ProveIt_Random_Tree_Stability.zip; batch 68: ProveIt_Hall_Bottlenecks.zip; batch 70:
ProveIt_Matching_Rank_Research.zip; batch 71: ProveIt_Weighted_Rank_Three_Leaf_Compression.zip
Proves Conjecture 5.1(c) of Athanasiadis and Chapoton (arXiv:2605.26916v1): for a preorder
τ on [n] the directed root polytope Cτ = conv({±ei } ∪ {ei − ej : j ≤τ i}) is reflexive with
h∗ (Cτ , t) = hτ (t), and any pulling triangulation of its boundary is combinatorially the boundary of
a simplicial polytope with that h-polynomial. Palindromicity and unimodality, Conjecture 5.1(a,b),
follow, as do the full g-theorem conditions. The higher-dimensional augmented bipartite identity
and preorder duality are credited to Dai, Hou, Liu, Thawinrak and Wang (arXiv:2608.16037v2).
Part II, added in batch 39, treats height-two preorders through matching polynomials: γ-positivity
in height two (Conjecture 5.2 there), a family showing that real-rootedness (Conjecture 5.3)
fails — its first counterexample is credited to a MathDB posting by Shivam Patel — and crown

                                                 35
obstructions: an induced crown forces large minimal nonfaces in every lattice triangulation of Cτ ,
so the boundary complex is not flag when the crown has at least six elements. Flag realizability
in general (Conjecture 5.1(d)) remains open. Part III, added in batch 42, shows that exactly the
cacti admit a faithful unit-phase minor model, a property strictly stronger than real stability.
Part IV, merged in batch 44 from two manuscripts, proves Conjecture 5.2 for every finite preorder:
γk counts ordered pairs of disjoint k-sets joined by a τ -increasing bijection, and a reflexive relation
gives a palindromic polynomial exactly when it is transitive. Part V, merged in batch 55 from
two manuscripts, shows that for      a bipartite graph with shores of sizes m and n every support
enumerator divided by min(m,n)
                                 
                             k     is log-concave (the unweighted inequality is Theorem A of Röhrle
and Ulirsch, arXiv:2402.15317, carried through the support-counting bridge), so every hτ and
every height-two γ-vector is ultra-log-concave; it classifies the palindromic support polynomials
of all bipartite graphs, gives FPRASes for partition functions and for single coefficients, and
shows exact counting #P-complete already in height two. Part VI, added in batch 55 with the
stability sections of a further manuscript, shows that stability of the signed support polynomial
is block-local, holds for the incidence–apex graph of a tree exactly when the tree is a finite or
affine ADE tree, and is classified for books and bipartite multi-theta graphs; the books show it
strictly stronger than real-rootedness. Part VII, added in batch 55, shows that the transversal
matroid of a preorder determines the preorder up to isomorphism and that height-two preorder
matroids generate exactly the gammoids under minors. Part VIII, added in batch 63, classifies
stability for one apex adjacent to an arbitrary set of vertices of a tree (a corollary of Part VI,
also proved directly), with exact O(n5 ) enumeration, optimization and repair of the stable apex
neighbourhoods, a sharp diameter window, and real-rootedness of their size-counting polynomial
exactly for paths. Part IX, added in batch 64, gives explicit polynomial-time two-flow bijections
between the demand vectors of a bipartite graph with prescribed support and the supplier sets
matchable to it (the correspondence itself is Oh’s), and with them an almost-uniform sampler
of actual lattice points of every finite preorder polytope. Part X, added in batch 66, answers
Part VIII’s question for a uniform random labelled tree with every vertex marked√independently
with probability p: the probability that the marking is stable is asymptotic to 98 2π p3 n5/2 e−pn
              √                                                                √
whenever p n → ∞, an explicit entire function governs the window p n → λ, and below it
stability is asymptotically the event of at most two marks; for a fixed host the diameter determines
the probability up to a polynomial factor, separating the typical rate log(1 − p) from the annealed
rate −p. Part XI, added in batch 68, answers Part V’s question on normalization by the matching
number negatively for positive vertex weights on both shores: a graph with 30 vertices, 104 edges,
matching number eight and weights in {1, 500} has a weighted support polynomial that is not
ultra-log-concave of order eight. A Hall-bottleneck family gives a rank-six phase diagram, the
endpoint threshold for every core size and a lower bound asymptotic to 43 ν for any normalization
depending on the matching number ν alone; the first rank-normalized inequality holds for every
positively weighted graph, with a defect identity. Part XII, added in batch 70, answers the
unit-weight version affirmatively for matching number at most three, by a sum-of-squares identity,
and for a connected family of matching number four and disjoint unions, leaving unit weights from
matching number four on and the one-shore version open. It also proves the first rank-normalized
inequality for weighted directed relations, gives a 9 × 9 rank-three bimatroid obstruction, exact
neighbourhood-type kernels with an exact support sampler, and for every finite preorder the first
                                   2d
gamma-vector inequality γ12 ≥ d−1     γ2 , with d the gamma degree, which also bounds the cost of
computing the gamma vector. Part XIII, added in batch 71, merges leaves with a common single
neighbour into one leaf of summed weight without changing the support polynomial, and so proves
normalization by the matching number for arbitrary positive weights on both shores whenever a
minimum vertex cover is split 1 + (ν − 1) between the shores, hence for every weighted graph of
matching number at most three. That left a positively weighted counterexample matching number
four, five or six, with every minimum vertex cover meeting both shores in at least two vertices,
and at matching number four, with or without weights, only covers split 2 + 2. Batch 72 continued
this line in a separate report, matching-rank-normalization (log-concavity section), to which dated


                                                  36
notes here now point: the unit-weight and one-shore versions fail (at matching numbers 3450
and 50), and the least matching number of a weighted failure is exactly six. Batch 73 opened
a second separate report, preorder-gamma-rank-ulc (log-concavity section), which answers this
report’s Research question 96, rank ultra-log-concavity of the gamma polynomial at its actual
degree, for unit activities through degree four and for independent activities through degree three.
Conjectures 7.2, 4.2 and 4.4 of the same paper are proved in the preorder-polytope reciprocity
report.
97. Largest Simplex Faces under Ordinal Sums
enumerative-combinatorics/
simplex-faces-of-order-and-chain-polytopes/Largest_Simplex_Faces.pdf
from Series_Parallel_Simplex_Faces_Research.zip
Answers the concluding open question of Freij-Hollanti and Lundström, Simplex inequalities of
order and chain polytopes of recursively defined posets (Order 43 (2026), article 31). For sO (P )
and sC (P ), the largest dimensions of simplex faces of the order and chain polytopes: both are
computable in O(n) on any series-parallel decomposition, the order case carrying eight normalized
endpoint states. For an ordinal sum of antichains, sO = r + ν with ν a maximum b-matching of
the layer path under capacities min(ai − 1, 2), and sC = r + max(0, k − 1). The extremal gap is
sharp: 0 ≤ sC − sO ≤ ⌊(n − 2)/3⌋, attained for every n ≥ 2.
98. Skew partitions, continued fractions and connected components
enumerative-combinatorics/skew-partition-continued-fractions/article.pdf
from A225114_proof_bundle.zip
Proves the repeated-power continued-fraction formula recorded as conjectural in OEIS A225114,
which counts skew partition diagrams with no empty row or column. Unique concatenation gives
A(q) = 1/(1 − P (q)) for the edge-connected diagrams, and a boundary-path encoding followed by a
weighted two-coloured Motzkin-path decomposition gives P (q) = q/(1 − 2q − q 3 /(1 − 2q 2 − q 5 /(1 −
2q 3 − · · · ))), whence A(q) = 1/(2 − C(q)) with C = 1 + P . Adds a four-variable refinement, a
formula by height, width and component count, a sharp truncation bound, exponential asymptotics
and a central limit theorem for the number of components. Explicitly not a novelty claim:
a prior-art check found Pasechnik’s MathOverflow sketch of ten September 2024, and the entry
credits Kurkov; the report says so first and credits both.

function is (P − Q  D)/(8x(1 + x) ). The headline structural result is the exact occurrence count
occ = f2 + U |U2 | , of which avoidance is the case occ = 0. Merged from two reports that reached

the identical generating function by the identical run-contraction substitution u 7→ u/(1 + xu);

100. Prescribed coefficient-lattice minima
enumerative-combinatorics/coefficient-lattice-minima/article.pdf
from coefficient_lattice_minima_research.zip
Settles the unnumbered realization conjecture after Lemma 1 of O’Bryant, On Nathanson’s
Triangular Number Phenomenon (arXiv:2506.20836): every nondecreasing list of even integers is
the list of successive L1 -minima of the coefficient lattice of some finite integer set. The witness is
the mixed-radix set A = {0, 1, h1 , h1 h2 , . . .}, whose minima are exactly (2h1 , . . . , 2hr ) with r + 2
distinct elements, and the radices may be given in any order.

                                                    37
101. Coefficientwise magic positivity through dimension ten
enumerative-combinatorics/magic-positivity-parking-polytopes/article.pdf
from magic_positivity_research.zip
A polytope is magic positive when its Ehrhart polynomial has nonnegative coefficients in the basis
tj (t + 1)n−j . Proves, in exact arithmetic, that the generalized parking-function polytope Xn (b)
is magic positive for every dimension 3 ≤ n ≤ 10 and every positive integer parameter vector,
uniformly in b rather than on a sampled box — a partial solution of Conjecture 8.1 of Hill, Luo,
Trinh and Vindas-Meléndez (arXiv:2607.15503v1). Dimension three is proved by printed algebraic
formulas; the first-coefficient harmonic-number formula and the endpoint identities hold in every
dimension. The conjecture in unrestricted dimension remains open.
102. Monotone blocks and reverse replies
enumerative-combinatorics/reverse-reply-avoidance-game/article.pdf
from reverse_reply_solution.zip
Proves Conjecture 2.9 of Ulfarsson (arXiv:2603.16004): for every k ≥ 3 and every n ≥ R(k) =
k 4 − 2k 3 − 3k 2 + 5k + 5, the second player wins the normal-play permutation-avoidance game on
Sn with pattern length k by always replying with the reversal, so the Sprague–Grundy value is 0.
The proof is elementary, with no computer search: a minimal-support lemma isolates one pattern
inside a family built from singletons plus one inflatable monotone interval, and inflation restores
the original length so the reply is legal. R(k) is asserted sufficient, not minimal.
103. Nonintegral tropical degree from two horizontal segments
enumerative-combinatorics/tropical-hilbert-nonintegral-degree/article.pdf
from tropical_hilbert_counterexample.zip
Computes the strict-witness tropical Hilbert function of Grigoriev (arXiv:2404.06440v2) exactly
for a two-segment set: THVd (k) = k + min{k, ⌈k/d⌉} + 1. Taking d = 3 gives tropical degree 4/3
— not an integer — and a quasipolynomial of least period 3, so eventual polynomiality fails. Every
rational in (1, 2] is realized, denominators are unbounded, and for d = p/q > 1 in lowest terms the
least eventual period is exactly p. Both bounds are proved by hand.
104. Finite-prefix corrections for polyomino growth
enumerative-combinatorics/polyomino-growth-finite-prefix-corrections/article.pdf
from ProveIt_Polyomino_Research.zip; batch 40:   ProveIt_Spectral_Saturation.zip
Continues the formal project Combinatorics/Polyominoes/KlarnerConstant, whose Lean-
verified bound is λ ≤ 9047/2000 = 4.5235 for Klarner’s constant. Proves a finite-prefix correction
theorem for proper positive polynomial recurrences and applies it to Bui’s seventeen-neighbourhood
system with an exact marked-occurrence table through size 18, giving the computer-assisted,
unformalized bound λ ≤ 2249/500 = 4.498 and An ≤ (927884613/109 )(2249/500)n . A second
theorem gives balanced integer-monomial divergence certificates, complete for productive, strongly
connected, nonlinear systems, and yields 4.52349 ≤ µoriginal ≤ 4.5235 for the original recurrence
— a statement about the recurrence, not a lower bound on λ. At intake, a fresh build of the
delivered enumerator (the same program, not an independent one) regenerated the size-18 table
and matched it exactly. Part II, added in batch 40, shows where the method stops: exact spectral
saturation in the irreducible critical case, a square-root law, and an all-prefix barrier µN ≥ 4.3149
for Bui’s map — a limit of this method, not a lower bound on λ and not an improvement on 4.498.
105. Three consecutive square-pyramidal numbers: exact Frobenius formulas
enumerative-combinatorics/a069762-pyramidal-frobenius/article.pdf
from batch 75:   OEIS_Pyramidal_Frobenius.zip
For sn = n(n + 1)(2n + 1)/6, gives the Frobenius number Fn of ⟨sn , sn+1 , sn+2 ⟩ (OEIS A069762,
which had no formula) exactly: a quintic quasipolynomial of least eventual period six, Fn =
n5 /9 + 8n4 /9 + O(n3 ), valid for every n ≥ 2 apart from six exceptions, the last at n = 23.
The proof is an explicit Apéry normal form after two gcd reductions. Also the exact genus,
quasipolynomials for every gap-power sum, a triangular limit law 2(1 − x) for a uniform gap
divided by Fn , the minimal generating-function denominator with an order-18 eventual recurrence,

                                                 38
and a convergent Puiseux inverse. Eventual quasipolynomiality itself is Roune–Woods’ and Shen’s
theorem; the claim is the explicit formula. The inversion instantiates the transseries volume’s
Lagrange–Bürmann and staircase theorems, which the report cites.
106. A pole hierarchy for valley-monotone compositions
enumerative-combinatorics/valley-monotone-bargraphs/article.pdf
from batch 75: OEIS_Valley_Monotone_Bargraphs.zip; batch 98:
OEIS_Bargraph_Critical_Windows_and_Extremes.zip (Part II)
Proves Conjecture 2.3 of Flórez, Ramírez and Villamizar (JCTA 2024): the number d(n) of composi-
tions of n with weakly increasing interior valley heights (non-decreasing bargraphs by area) satisfies
d(n) = Cλn (1 + O(θn )) with θ < 1, λ = 1.97642067794583 . . . and C = 0.60390151906189 . . . certi-
fied by rational interval arithmetic; the digits printed in 2024 are the finite ratio at n = 100. The
input is the stacks generating function of OEIS A001523; d(n) itself has no verified OEIS number.
Also infinitely many simple real poles increasing to 1, so the generating function is not D-finite, a
finite-pole expansion to every radius below 1, a central limit theorem for bottom-level valleys, a
terminal-component law and the asymptotics of the pole accumulation. Whether the real poles
exhaust the spectrum is left open. Part II (batch 98) answers the extreme-height half of Part I’s
question 10.4: the tallest column Hn satisfies P (Hn ≤ h) = exp(−αnρh )(1 + O(log n/n)) uniformly
in the central window, α = 0.45020217246 . . ., with lattice limits along phase subsequences and no
single continuous Gumbel limit, asymptotically independent of the Gaussian bottom-valley count.
Weighting bottom-level valleys by u makes their count geometric below, uniform after scaling at
and Gaussian above the exact critical weight uc = ρ2 (1 − ρ2 )(1 − ρ22 )/(1 + ρ42 ) = 0.12218100900 . . .
(ρ2 the second real pole), with a uniform 1/n critical window to all orders, Dirichlet area fractions
at several simultaneous critical levels, an exact q-product identity for the height tail, a fixed-order
expansion of the height-cutoff pole, a Dirichlet mixture of Gumbel-type maxima at criticality, and
Lambert-W inverses that instantiate the transseries volume. Five statements the manuscript only
sketched are proved in the write; the independent check found them valid and widened the range
on which one lemma’s proof gives uniqueness. The joint normal law for the additive statistics
of question 10.4 stays open, and Part II’s decimals are uncertified diagnostics. Unrefereed; not
formalized.

108. A finite positive amplitude for stressed Kunz words
enumerative-combinatorics/numerical-semigroup-stressed-amplitude/article.pdf
from Report274_Stressed_Kunz_Finite_Amplitude.zip (Report 274, arrival 764740f07, outside the
numbered batches)
Numerical semigroups by genus in Kunz coordinates. Claims that the stressed words on {1, 2, 3}
satisfy sg = Cs ρg + O(ρg2 ) with 0 < Cs < ∞, 1/ρ the root of (x2 + x3 )(x + x2 + x3 ) = 1, so that
Zhu’s polynomial-prefactor formulation (his Conjecture 7.3) holds for sg with exponent 0, against
his numerical expectation of 1.6–1.7; also a two-pole expansion for depth at most three, a shrinking
inverse bracket and the vanishing of every fixed inverse-power correction. The proof is self-

                                                   39
dense-window standardization, rational certificates and early-one suppression. Not independently

still pre-asymptotic, and the source itself asks for an independent check, recorded as a further
question; the check split Zhu’s counts exactly into the separated and the early-one classes. It sits
beside numerical-semigroup-leaf-types (the same objects, no shared theorem); Reports 270
and 271, which it names, were not delivered. Unrefereed; not formalized.

109. A Mixed-Base Counterexample to Stanley’s Rationality Question
generating-functions-and-asymptotics/binary-product-coefficient-counts/
mixed-base-counterexample/article.pdf
from stanley_mixed_base_counterexample.zip + Stanley_Rationality_Counterexample.zip
With G2j+1 = 2j and G2j+2 = 3j the exponent generating function is rational, while ν(2n) =
2 n−⌊(n+1)α⌋ for α = log3 2 counts the coefficients equal to one in i≤N (1 + xGi ) and its generating
                                                                   Q

function is not rational, not D-finite, not P-recursive, and has a natural boundary. Classifies
rationality in the family interleaving aj and q j with digit polynomial 1 + y + · · · + y a−1 for all
2 ≤ a < q — Stanley’s general r = a − 1 — as holding exactly when q u = av . Adds a finite-quotient
rigidity theorem, an abstract natural-boundary theorem for q ⌊nθ⌋ tn at every irrational θ, the
                                                                  P

exact limit set of an /λn , and a streaming recurrence making the point that non-P-recursive does
not mean hard to compute. Merged; the enumerative half was the same argument in both originals,
but the two routes to non-rationality differ and both are kept — an elementary finite-state reduction
mod p, and the rigidity route that reaches the stronger transcendence conclusions.
110. Rational exponents, nonrational coefficient counts
generating-functions-and-asymptotics/binary-product-coefficient-counts/
recursive-exponent-construction/research.pdf
from stanley_counterexample.zip
A different negative answer, from the recursion a0 = 2, a1 = 1, an = an−1 − 2an−2 , with
bn = Tn−1 + 2n − 1 + an , Tn = Tn−1 + bn , G2n−1 = 2n−1 and G2n = bn : the counting series has a
natural boundary on the unit circle, hence is neither rational, algebraic nor D-finite.
111. Coefficient residues in generalized Fibonacci products
generating-functions-and-asymptotics/binary-product-coefficient-counts/
kbonacci-coefficient-residues/article.pdf
from stanley_kbonacci_research.zip + quasifibonacci_parity_research.zip
Settles Conjectures 6.2 and 6.3 of Stanley, Theorems and Conjectures on Some Rational Generating
Functions (arXiv:2101.02131v3, pp. 23–24). For k-bonacci seeds each exceeding the sum of
their predecessors, the number of odd coefficients of i≤n (1 + xwi ) has generating function
                                                            Q

(1 + 2z k )/(1 − 2z + 2z k − 2z k+1 ), independently of the seeds; the proof builds periodic signs of
block length k + 1 and block product −1 and shows every signed finite product is flat. Merged; the
Conjecture 6.3 halves proved the same statement on the same domain, and the surviving report
adds Conjecture 6.2 and the asymptotic analysis.

12.2 Single-sequence OEIS studies
One hundred and forty-four reports on the growth or the closed form of a single OEIS sequence.
                                                                             x    2
One article counts the terms in the derivatives of three power towers — xx , xx and xx — whose
three parts reach three different answers by three different recurrences, and two reports added in
                                                                  a
batch 72 sit beside it: one proves the leading constant 12 for xx at every integer a, hence for xx
        2                               2
and xx , and the other studies the xx coefficients at unbounded logarithmic deficit. Two more
reports share a Bell-scale saddle-point method on unrelated sequences. Shared technique is not
shared theorem, and none of these duplicates another. Thirteen reports came in batch 73, one in
batch 74, one in batch 75, thirty-three in batch 77, five in batch 85, one in batch 91, seventy-eight
in batches 100–113 and one in batch 138; they are the last one hundred and thirty-three entries
below, in order of placement, the batch-77 ones grouped by the cluster that placed them (ascent
sequences; iterated Bell numbers, cone walks and divisor weights; trees and networks; words,
permutations, partitions and placements; moments, renewals and moving parameters). Four
batch-73 reports merge two manuscripts that proved the same theorems independently on the
same day (A139217, A022629, A215561, A279619): the shared theorems are printed once and the
other proofs kept as marked second routes. Batch 75 brought a third and a fourth independent
proof of the A022629 theorems, and a manuscript about two thirds of which re-proves results of the
A215561 report; those duplicates are tabulated in the reports, not reprinted, and only their new
results were added. Batch 77 did the same with a fifth independent proof of the A022629 theorems,
a fourth of the A215561 ones, a third of the A279619 constant and a second of the A126764
corrections. Several of its new reports join a manuscript with addenda that nest it byte for byte;
each paper is printed once. The four partition reports of batches 73–74 (A022629, A033552,
A097356, A238016) study different products in different saddle regimes and share no proposition,
nor do the batch-77 partition reports (A174065, A291698, A292507, A301746, A301981), which
share at most a method with them; the A189281 and A330266 reports share a method but no
theorem. The two batch-85 manuscripts on matrix compositions proved the same core theorems
independently and are one report, printed in one notation with a dictionary; batches 85 and 86

114. Dyadic scaling rigidity of 3AP-free permutations

For θ(n), the number of permutations of [n] with no three-term arithmetic progression (OEIS
A003407), studies the dyadic scaling of θ(n): a continuous log-periodic growth profile with its
exact fundamental period, rigidity of the profile under real rescaling, explicit losses and gains of the
2:1 gluing, and the empirical and sampling laws; the adjacent ratios θ(n + 1)/θ(n) have no limit.
The parity recurrences and Sharma’s Theorem 2.8, which the manuscript imports, are already
proved in Lean in Combinatorics/Ramsey (the table θ(1..20) there rests on a native_decide
certificate); the report’s own theorems are not formalized. Part II, added in batch 43, proves θ is
not P-recursive and its generating functions are not D-finite (the ordinary one is transcendental),
using the published counts θ(64), θ(75), which nothing in ProveIt certifies; whether the generating
function has a natural boundary stays open.

116. The maximal positive coefficient of a Hermite polynomial

from A277280_asymptotics.zip
An exact closed form for OEIS A277280, the largest
                                               √ coefficient of the physicists’ Hermite polynomial
Hn with signs retained: with kn = ⌊(2n + 6 − ⌊ 8n + 21⌋)/8⌋ and dn = n − 4kn , the exponent dn
is the unique maximizer and a(n) = n! 2dn /((2kn )! dn !). The distinction from the largest coefficient
in absolute value is kept explicit throughout.

122. Distinct third derivatives of a power tower
generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/
a199085-exponentiation-derivative-values/article.pdf
from A199085_generating_function.zip
For OEIS A199085, the number of distinct values taken by the third derivative at x = 1 over all
fully parenthesized exponentiations of n copies of x, proves a(1) = a(2) = 1 and a(n) = ⌊(n2 −2)/3⌋
for n ≥ 3, with the rational generating function z/(1 − z) + z 3 /(1 − z)3 − z 7 /((1 − z)2 (1 − z 3 )).
The proof is for all n, not an extrapolation: every attainable pair of second and third derivatives
is classified and a witness constructed for each admissible pair. Despite the shared vocabulary
this is a different question from power-tower-derivative-term-counts, which fixes the function and
varies the derivative order; here the order is fixed at three and the parenthesization varies.
123. Finite-fringe rigidity in congruence-restricted greedy dissociated sequences

from batch 73:   finite_fringe_oeis_A139217_A139218.zip + ProveIt_OEIS_A139217_A139218.zip
OEIS A139217 and A139218 choose greedily the least positive integer congruent to 1, respectively
2, modulo 3 that keeps the set dissociated (all subset sums distinct). After three terms the

                                                  45
and {1, 3, 6, 11} for A139218, and a general propagation theorem keeps it so forever. Hence

route, and the cross-identities, remainder bound and tables that only one has are added.

value, from a contour-controlled saddle estimate, a Barnes G product identity and a Fourier
series in Γ(s)ζ(1 + s); also a phase-dependent limit law for the largest part index. The entry
poses no asymptotic conjecture, and the methods are classical. Part II (batch 98) merges two
independent manuscripts answering Part I’s questions 10.4 and 10.5, with the more general one
as base: the exact total-variation threshold for independence of the upper multiplicities from
geometric variables; a corrected edge law P (Jn − M ≤ h) = Gh (1 + Ch /m) + O(m−2 ) for the
largest index, proved in both and printed once, with growing offsets, two-sided tails, all moments
and periodic first corrections; for the number of parts, tn Nn tends to an√explicit series W of scaled
exponential variables with no 1/m term in the mean (S1 = 1 + 4π/(9 3)); joint limits with the
upper process; the law of W , whose tail series has the unit circle as natural boundary, with an
inverse tail; a Brownian-bridge mass profile and a lacunary universality class. The write proved
one manuscript’s unproved covariance candidate, m Cov(tn Nn , Jn ) → −S1 Ξ(θ); the independent
check found it valid and sketched a sharper remainder. No claim of either manuscript was found
wrong; the conditioning framework is classical (Arratia–Tavaré, Fristedt), and no exponentially
small sectors or interval certificates are claimed. Unrefereed; not formalized.

OEIS A189281 and A110128 count permutations avoiding a prescribed signed or absolute difference

flat remainder e an /n! − C∗ (n) that is nonzero at least once in every four consecutive indices;
two numerical statements of its source (a truncation index, and one sign change that is in fact
fourteen) are corrected on record. Part VI (batch 101, bundle Report 83), a same-day third
derivation of Part I’s theorem that predates Parts I–II, prints only its additions: exact Bonferroni
enclosures at n = 100, 200, 1000, the threshold ceiling bracket and an exact-digamma inverse

C2 , in which cycles enter first at order n−2 ; A189358 (camel) = e−4 n!(1 + 8/n + 48/n2 + O(n−3 ))

For an = k k n k! S(n, k) (OEIS A122399), the diagonal of 1/(1−uey (ex −1)): an exact, absolutely
         P

convergent vertical-contour formula for the two-size numbers Am,n with a nonasymptotic tail

records; the write proved a sketched degree bound and the sharpness of the count half of the
growing Poisson window, and the independent check corrected that proof’s closing sentence, which
named an impossible regime. Large deviations, count asymptotics for m/n → ∞ and a bridge to
the saddle range stay open. Unrefereed; not formalized.

Θ((16/3)n n−αS ) with the conjectured αP = 1 + π/ arccos(9/16) and αS = 1 + π/ arccos(22/27),
by a one-unit cycle buffer, a random-clock transfer and a Fourier local limit; since both exponents
are irrational, the generating functions of A377922, A377920 and A377921 are not D-finite; and

walks on Z2 × E with a finite phase set E and unbounded steps with a uniform exponential
moment, killed on leaving the quadrant: under hypotheses (H1)–(H5) the exact-time kernel is
n−1−π/θ+o(1) , with a survival bound under (H1)–(H4). Also new: a six-face sleeve injection giving

logarithmic law alone (stated in general at the write); the exponential-moment certificate 189/13;

weaker second route, and its open amplitudes, ratio limit and O(1) inverse are settled by Parts I–II
(dated notes). a348351-one-sided-rectangulations proves its exponent by the same method;
its walk satisfies (H1)–(H5). The independent check found the write’s remarks valid and corrected
a scale label and a coefficient in one proof. An amplitude or Θ form of the cone theorem stays
open. Unrefereed; not formalized.

For Sn (r) = k Mn (k)r , with Mn (k) the number of permutations of n with k inversions, proves
             P

that for every rn → ∞ the ratio Sn (rn )/Cnrn , Cn the central Mahonian number, is asymptotic

(r = 4), is in Wang’s 2026 manuscript and is not claimed; priority for the growing-power extension
is not established. A dated note relates the crossover to the critical theorem of the theta-ballot
report; that report’s general lattice-peak question is answered in a sufficient-hypothesis form by
its Part II (batch 98), whose Proposition 21.1 makes this report’s critical window an instance by
reorganizing its proof.

157. Full support signed permutations and their asymptotic coefficients
generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/
a260952-full-support-signed-permutations/article.pdf
from batch 77:   Coxeter_Full_Support_Asymptotics.zip
Proves Kotěšovec’s 2015 conjecture recorded in OEIS A260952, am ∼ −2m+1 m!/(9(log 3)m+1 ),
with the corrections 1−2τ /m−9τ 2 /(4(m)2 )−27τ 3 /(2(m)3 )−1755τ 4 /(16(m)4 )+O(m−5 ), τ = log 3,
every further fixed order by a finite formula, and am < 0 for all m ≥ 1. The am are the asymptotic

of the transseries volume. A companion of the A260700 report above; neither uses the other.

Counts sets and multisets of rotation classes of words of fixed length ℓ ≥ 3 over an n-letter alphabet

                                            2 2

                                   P

cannot be replaced by 1. The proof uses mean-matching exponential tilts and a sharp global

168. Complete increasing subsequences in multiset permutations (A047909 and
A268485)
generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/
a047909-beta-renewal-subsequences/article.pdf
from batch 77: beta-renewal-asymptotics-reproducibility.zip +
beta-renewal-uniform-reproducibility.zip
Studies the probability pm (k) = H(m, k)(m!)k /(mk)! that a word with m copies of each of
1, . . . , k contains 12 · · · k (OEIS A047909, diagonal A268485). Its representation as the probability
that k independent Beta(1, m) increments sum to at most 1, and the Gaussian limit, are El
Maazouz–Pitman’s and are credited, not claimed. Part I proves an expansion to every fixed order
                                   √
in the window k = m + x m with P1 , . . . , P4 explicit (P1 = (x2 + 8)/6); on the diagonal only
odd half-integer powers occur beyond the leading 1/2, with four corrections computed. It adds
smooth quantile and Lambert-W count inverses with qualified integer brackets and all-orders
rare-tail expansions for k/m in compact subsets of (0, 1) or (1, ∞). Part II, an addendum, joins
the regimes: relative error O(m−(R+1)/2 ) for both tails, uniformly on compact ranges of k/m and
through k = m + 1, recovering Part I’s coefficients by a second route, and both real Lambert-W
branches for tail thresholds. The inverses are instances of the transseries volume and the tilting
method is classical (Esscher, Cramér, Daniels). Effective constants, growing orders, ratios tending
to 0 or ∞, and certified rounding remain open.
169. Uniform asymptotics and moving zeros for the level-15 family of Cooper
generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/
cooper-level-15-moving-zeros/article.pdf
from batch 77:   modular-crossover-replay.zip
For Cooper’s level-15 polynomials Tε (n), of degree n in the parameter ε, proves a two-singularity
expansion with rates ε + 11 and ε − 1, uniform to every fixed order on the disc |ε + 5| ≤ 1/10,
with an additive error that stays valid where the two terms cancel; in the window ε = −5 + u/n
the profile is eu/6 + 10(−1)n e−u/6 . Hence for every large odd n there is exactly one zero with
|u − 3 log 10| < 1, real and simple, at εn = −5 + 3 log(10)/n + (162/125 − 92 log 10)/n2 + O(n−3 ),
and no real zero in a bounded window for large even n. Explicit path, sheet and connection proofs
give all ten constants of Cooper’s Table 8 (levels 11, 14, 15 and 24), labelled conjectured and
determined numerically there, including two negative-axis connections with exact eta multipliers.
The formulas are Cooper’s, the level-11 equivalent is Kotěšovec’s (A284756) and the positive
modular radial principle Guillera–Zudilin’s. Also a finite generator for every correction order and
a qualified, non-effective threshold inverse, an instance of the transseries volume. No historical
priority and no global location of the zeros are claimed; the OEIS numbers of five of the sequences
were not verified.
170. Moving fugacity asymptotics for distinct partitions
generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/

from batch 77:   moving-fugacity-reproducibility.zip
For an (α) = [q n ] k≥1 (1 + nα q k ), distinct partitions with one size-dependent weight u = nα per
                   Q

part, proves, uniformly for α in compact subsets of (0, ∞), an all-orders principal Bessel expansion
with a finite generator for its coefficients and, through an exact Jacobi decomposition, finitely
many conjugate resonance sectors with separated local integrals and sectorwise remainders. This
yields the equivalents conjectured by Kotěšovec for A291698 (α = 1) and A292304 (α = 2), and
shows that the elementary Sommerfeld form is relatively valid exactly when α ≥ 1/2. Also a

171. Gamma constants and scaling limits for rounding extinction
generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/

from batch 85: Rounding_Extinction_OEIS.zip; batch 110:

Starting from x1 = n and rounding down to a multiple of k m at stages k = 2, 3, . . ., the first zero
satisfies τm (n) ∼ (cm n)1/(m+1) with cm = mΓ(m/(m + 1))m+1 for every real m > 0: Cloitre’s
conjecture in OEIS A082528, with the constants 2Γ(2/3)3 and 3Γ(3/4)4 conjectured numerically
for A082527 and A082528. The case m = 1 (c1 = π, the Tchoukaillon sieve A002491) is Erdős and
Jabotinsky’s and is credited, not claimed. Also the uniform limit of the whole extinction trajectory,
the same law for weights with k(wk /wk−1 − 1) → m, rational product certificates for cm , the
limiting distribution of rounding losses, the dependence on m, and a block construction showing
that regular variation alone does not fix the constant. No error term (the O(1) recorded in A073047
is not proved), no rate, nothing uniform in a moving m; Appendix B is an OEIS update draft that
was not posted, and the OEIS terms the package uses are CC BY-SA 4.0 data. Part II (batch 110,
                                                                                                2

uniformly on compact p-sets, through a monotone integral coordinate and dyadic telescoping, with

law: it answers Part I’s question on rates, its leading law is a second route to Part I’s Theorem 1.1,
and the write corrects the last digits of its decimals c2 , c3 and its stale novelty sentences. Part III
(batch 110, from a non-bundle archive held from batch 114) answers the question on schedules for
nondecreasing weights with bounded scaled increments and a convergent augmented law — finite
Gamma products, the activation jump 1/(2π) → π/8, a schedule with no limit, the exact spectrum
(0, 1/(m + 1)), Möbius recovery — and the bounded form of the sparse-exception question; sparse

174. Finite Fredholm truncation for long increasing subsequences
generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/
a047874-long-increasing-subsequences/article.pdf
from batch 85:   LIS_fixed_sector_research_package.zip
For the permutations of N letters with longest increasing subsequence of length exactly k (OEIS
A047874) or at least k (A214152), truncating a Bonferroni expansion over shifted rows of the
Robinson–Schensted shape at R terms gives globally holonomic arrays that agree with the true
ones for N < (R + 1)(k + R), a cutoff shown sharp, so both arrays are D-finite in every fixed
sector N ≤ rk: the sector question that Kauers and Wang (arXiv:2609.02220) raise informally,
whose case r = 2 is their theorem. Hence P-recursiveness along every rational ray; single sums
when N ≤ 2k + 1; a uniform all-orders expansion of the tail on compact ranges of (N − k)/k, with
polynomial coefficients; and five correction terms for A269021 (whose leading term is Kotěšovec’s)
and three for A267433. The holonomic-closure step is flagged for independent review; the guessed
operators of the one-third sector, minimal orders and global holonomicity are not claimed, and
the discrete Bessel kernel and holonomic summation are imported. Not formalized; OEIS fixtures
are CC BY-SA 4.0 data.

has minimal order and that A181199 obeys no recurrence of order one; the independent check

October 2026, before Parts I–II and without knowledge of them), earlier independent routes to

the all-orders expansion, the algebraicity dichotomy and the rational-diagonal theorem; the report

generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/

For an , the column-convex permutominoes with n rows and n columns (OEIS A196275), a

here only against the 200 b-file terms) into bn = an /(n + 1)! = 12 ⟨1, T n 1⟩ for a positive self-
adjoint operator T on L2 [0, 1] with an elementary resolvent. Hence the closed form B(z) =
−(2 − z) log(1 − z)/(2z(2 − z + log(1 − z))), an exact spectral measure (one atom at h =
1/(1 − W0 (1/e)) ≈ 1.38593 off [0, 1] plus a positive density on [0, 1]), and an ∼ k (n + 1)! hn with
k = (2h − 1)(h − 1)/2 ≈ 0.34191; the source presents this as a proof of the equivalent conjectured

expansion to every order and a mixed inverse-power/inverse-logarithmic expansion with explicit
coefficient functions and sector-level remainders; for a specified spectral interpolation the inverse is
a convergent series of exponentially small sectors about the exact Gamma-envelope inverse; with
E(x) = k Γ(x + 2)hx , E(n) < an < E(n + 12 ) for every n ≥ 1, so rounding E −1 (an ) recovers every
index; and B is not D-finite, so (an ) is not P-recursive. The write proves the disk estimate behind
the convergent inverse series (Proposition 8.2) and an L2 statement on the     √ Bernstein-smoothed
profile: its distance from the projection on the eigenvector at h is exactly 2r2n → 0 (the write’s
bound ≤ 1, sharpened by the independent check). Not claimed: local asymptotics of the profile
coefficients, optimal truncation, convergence of the logarithmic series, interval-certified inversion
or global priority; the tail estimates of Theorems 5.1 and 6.1 are written in condensed form. The
inversion steps are instances of the transseries volume’s factorial-core and staircase theorems.

189. Inversion sequences solved by kernel orbits (OEIS A279544, A279567, A279569,

Britt and Beaton (arXiv:2512.21943v3) gave generating trees and catalytic equations for the
inversion sequences avoiding a triple of relations and stated, explicitly as non-rigorous, laws
an ∼ Cµn n−3/2 for four classes. Three manuscripts prove them by one method: a kernel
substitution reduces the catalytic equation to a scalar affine functional equation, solved along
contracting Möbius-type orbits seeded at an algebraic kernel root; a global continuation argument
excludes competing singularities, and an exact rational certificate shows that the square-root
amplitude does not vanish. Part I √      treats class 214 (OEIS A279544, µ = 4, C to 52 decimals)
and class 1509 (A279567, µ = 3 + 2 2); Part II class 1953A (A279569, µ = 27/4) and, through

and a threshold inverse inside two ceilings, centred at a Lambert-W−1 root in Parts I–II and
expanded in powers of 1/ log M with coefficients polynomial in log log M in Part III; the inverses
are instances of the transseries volume. Kotěšovec’s OEIS amplitudes agree with every certified
interval, so no digits are new. Nonalgebraicity and D-finiteness (which Britt and Beaton believe
fail), the corrections beyond c2 , effective constants (the inverse constants are existential) and
earlier rigorous literature (not searched) stay open. Nothing in the manuscripts was found wrong;
the independent check corrected the write’s statement that every Part proves a Lambert-W−1
inverse. Siblings from the same paper, by unrelated methods: a279571-inversion-cone-walk
and a279551-inversion-log-deficit. Merged from three manuscripts that share no passage;
every Part is printed in full. Unrefereed; not formalized.

197. Alternating Baxter involutions (OEIS A347546): the corrected count and its

OEIS A347546 counts the involutions among the doubly alternating Baxter permutations of length
n. Part I shows that the recurrence of Min (J. Chungcheong Math. Soc. 34 (2021), Theorem 2.7),
from which the OEIS data were computed, miscounts this class: in an even-length involution the
two outer blocks are mutual inverses, not involutions, so the outer factor is the Catalan number
Cj , not the involution count oj , and C4 = 14 > 12 = o4 . The class has 2168 members of length 20,
not 2166; two omitted involutions are printed. From three published necessity lemmas of Min and
Park (2006), accepted rather than re-proved, Part I proves
                                                         √      the corrected recurrences,√ algebraic
generating functions with dominant singularity ρ = ( 5 − 1)/4, equivalents As (1 + 5)m m−3/2
for the even and odd subsequences with every fixed order, and Lambert-W−1 length inverses with
ceiling brackets leaving at most two adjacent candidates. Its Catalan count of doubly alternating
Baxter permutations is Guibert and Linusson’s theorem (2000), re-derived and credited at the
write. Part II refines the count by pairs of fixed points (q = 1 gives Part I back): every fixed order
uniformly on compact complex windows of the sparse weight q = w/m with entire profiles, an exact
criterion for inverting in the weight, interior inverses, and Poisson-type laws with total-variation
rate O(m−1/2 ). The write proves that Min’s odd formula is right and her even formula fails exactly
from half-index 10 on, so the recurrence-defined sequence lies strictly below the class at n = 20
and every n ≥ 22: Min’s printed values at n = 20, 22, . . . , 26 and 21 of the 42 terms of A347546
(n = 20 and 22 ≤ n ≤ 41) are wrong, while its definition is right; nothing was sent to the OEIS
or the journal. The independent check confirmed the six corrected values of Min’s list as direct
counts (literal brute force through n = 26). The leading Lambert centres are instances of the
transseries volume. No effective constants, all-index monotonicity or fixed-weight central limit
theorem. Unrefereed; not formalized.

explicit) and C ≈ 1.0986858055 (uncertified), directly from the Stirling recurrence: a factorial

norm 2 log 2 − 1 < 1. The same argument on the disk |q − 1| ≤ 1/16 of the length marker
gives every fixed cumulant of the chain length. It adds a Lambert-W0 inverse with a triangular
recursion, eventual recovery of n from log zn , and two-ceiling threshold enclosures. Against the
transseries volume (write): the core is an instance of its factorial core, not of its Lambert core,
the recursion of its core reversion, the recovery of its staircase rounding; the enclosure is proved
directly, with a second route through the staircase theorem. The leading equivalent and C > 0

line “E.g.f. satisfies Z(z) = 12 (Z(ez − 1) − z)” (in the entry since 2003) has the wrong sign: the
correct equation is 2Z(z) = z + Z(ez − 1); nothing was sent to the OEIS. A formal Laplace link

question; the independent check traced its 1.9 · 10−4 numerical gap to the (log n)2 /n term of

For a(n), the number of inclusion-maximal families of pairwise disjoint strict partitions of n
(OEIS A068598), proves 1/5 ≤ lim inf log a(n)/(n log n) ≤ lim sup ≤ 1/4. The upper bound
a(n) ≤ 3n−1 n⌊(n−2)/4⌋ (n ≥ 2) is elementary and explicit, from an anchor and free-label encoding
and the distinct-parts mass inequality (Spencer, via Nathanson); the lower constant 1/5 injects
queens configurations and rests on Luria and Simkin’s theorem, while an elementary construction
gives 1/7. Also first-passage inverses with constants between 4 and 5 and finite Lambert brackets,

                                                 85
without monotonicity. Two statements of the OEIS entry are corrected (nothing submitted):
Meeussen’s graph comment counts maximal cliques (at n = 8: six vertices, 21 nonempty complete
subgraphs, a(8) = 3), and Beregovsky’s fits A exp(Bnc ) with c > 1 (2022, 2026) are not asymptotic:
each exceeds the proved upper bound for n ≥ 1215 resp. n ≥ 8579, signs certified in ball arithmetic
at the independent check. The write adds Simkin’s 2023 improvement of the lower bound’s linear
term (read at abstract level), the two Lambert roots as instances of the transseries volume’s
factorial core and the brackets as analogues only, and an exact free-label identity that locates the
families any proof of the constant 1/4 would have to count. The independent check corrected the
write’s Simkin interval to [0.7096, 0.7109] and one sentence on the block construction. No limit,
equivalent or expansion; the linear term belongs to a lower bound only; the monotonicity of a(n)
and the count of quadruple decompositions are open. Unrefereed; not formalized.

algebraicity and faster enumeration
generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/
a307030-pop-stacked-permutations/article.pdf
from batch 108: Pop_Stacked_Permutations_Exact_Generating_Function_and_Poles_Source.zip (bundle

For pop-stacked permutations (OEIS A307030; by runs A309993), the base, bundle Report 164,
derives from the run-overlap characterization an explicit exponential generating function, a
quotient P √ = N/D of explicit entire functions (near 0, a ratio of sin(zr/4), cos(zr/4) terms
with r = 4ez − 1). Every genuine pole is simple, with residue −2e−2ζ (4eζ − 1)/(ζ + 2); there
are infinitely many positive poles, so P is not D-finite and (pn ) is not P-recursive, as Claesson,
Guðmundsson and Pantone (CGP, arXiv:1908.08910) predicted numerically; yet P is differentially
algebraic (an annihilator of order at most two, minimality not claimed). A Riccati equation
computes p0 , . . . , pN in O(N 2 ) arithmetic operations against O(N 4 ) in the cited papers (not a
bit-complexity claim). The dominant pole ρ = 1.1134390417 . . . and amplitude C = 2e−2ρ (4eρ −
1)/(ρ(ρ + 2)) = 0.6956885490 . . . are rationally certified and confirm CGP’s decimals. Report 161
is a second route to pn = Cn!ρ−n (1 + O(q n )) by a positive-operator argument without the formula,

                                                  86
with the elementary bound (24/23)1/4 ≤ ρ ≤ 2 and all fixed inverse orders. Report 166 adds
the bivariate generating function, run mean µn + β (µ = 0.44214954918886 . . .) and variance
vn + γ, a central and an O(1/n) lattice local limit theorem, and an alternate proof of CGP’s
Conjecture 2 (fixed-run denominators); it credits a prior proof posted by Shivam Patel on MathDB
(20 August 2026), which the write read and checked: Patel’s proof is first, Report 166’s a different
one. The write proves (Remark 5.1, interval arithmetic) that CGP’s printed list of positive poles
(Section 3.3, p. 10) skips ρ12 = 5.33887671271979 . . ., every printed digit of the other fifteen
being right (their repository file lists ρ12 ), and finds their three printed complex pairs correct
to 62–66 decimals, after which each drops two digits; it credits Part II’s interleaving bound to
Asinowski–Banderier–Hackl’s Theorem 12 and moves twelve open items (effective gap and onsets,
nonreal poles, a minimal ODE, bit complexity, certified β, γ, among others) to its questions. The
independent check re-proved Remark 5.1 by its own phase argument, confirmed Patel’s proof step
by step and the recurrence against all 450 b-file terms, and corrected the write’s description of
ABH’s Theorem 11 (its statement does include the costs Part I cites) and of Patel’s MathDB status.
No effective gap, error constants or onsets, no pole-sum expansion, no certified β, γ; OEIS terms
are CC BY-SA 4.0. Merged from three manuscripts in batch 108. Unrefereed; not formalized.

                    √

              √                                                                             √
          2
an ∼ (n!) e 2   2n /(25/4 πn3/4 ) with an expansion to every fixed order in n−1/2 : c1 = −17 2/96, c2 =
6409/9216, c3 and c4 explicit, and a finite exact Gaussian-moment rule for every later coefficient,
plus a logarithmic expansion. The route is the labelled-component√decomposition (isolated vertices,
paths, even cycles) and a modified-Bessel diagonal G(t) = P (t)I0 ( t(2 − t)/(1 − t)), which satisfies

exponentially small terms, existential remainder constants and thresholds; rectangular shapes

line from the A062154 formula, proves the cycles-only ratio to A001499, and extends the proof
to An+d,n for fixed d (all orders) and to the zero rows and columns, asymptotically independent

A matching of a complete multipartite graph is maximal exactly when its unmatched vertices lie
in one part, so every count is a sum ofpfactorial ratios. Part I treats An , the maximal matchings
of Kn,n,n (OEIS A297487): with λ = n/2, an expansion to every fixed order, uniform √         in a real
                                                                             √
marker of unmatched vertices, which gives the OEIS equivalent An ∼ 3 2(2n/e)           3n/2 e n/2−3/8
with four corrections; the unmatched count is within O(n       −1/4 ) in total variation of a parity-
conditioned Poisson(λ) law; the probability of a maximum matching to every fixed order; and a
two-ceiling threshold bracket. Part II, an explicit sequel, treats unequal parts: the branches for
strictly triangular proportions, and K2n+d,n,n across the face a = b + c of the triangle inequality
with a uniform moving window at scale n1/3 , Gaussian laws, four corrections of the boundary
counts M (2n, n, n) (no OEIS entry) and an exterior Poisson law. It settles one instance of Part I’s
question on unequal parts; more parts and higher orders stay open. The write compared all
100 b-file terms, proved Howroyd’s OEIS program equal to the exact formula, and classified the

224. Distinct sums of square roots: quantitative asymptotics and inverse indices
(OEIS A397045)
generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/

from batch 109:   Distinct_Square_Root_Sums_Asymptotics_and_Inverses_Source.zip (bundle Report 172)
a(n), the number of distinct values in [n, n + 1) of finite sums of square roots of positive √ integers
(OEIS A397045), equals the number of vectors (md ) over the squarefree d ≥ 2 with md d < n+1.
                                                                                       P

Proves log a(n) = K(n + 1)2/3 + O(n1/3 ), K = (81ζ(3)/π 2 )1/3 , which is the entry’s conjecture
including the constant (Kohlbecker’s 1958 Tauberian theorem already implies the leading law,
as the report says and the write works out), an exact-product saddle formula with relative error
O(n−1/3 ), inverse indices, and, unconditionally, that no equivalent Cnβ exp(Kn2/3 ) exists, because
of a surviving pole at a zeta zero of least height. The write refutes, with proof, the entry’s

is 3, 8, 21, 46, 104 against a(n) = 3, 7, 17, 35, 76), proves that a(n) increases strictly, and extends

255. Distinct distances in a cubic lattice (OEIS A275672)
generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/
