from batch 138:   A275672_Research.zip (arrival 2c7f4fd68)
OEIS A275672 (Vladimir Reshetnikov’s entry of 2016) is the largest number of points of {0, . . . , n−
1}3 whose pairwise Euclidean distances are all different. Exhaustive diameter-case C++ searches
with verified witnesses give the new terms a(7) = 10 and a(8) = 12 (computation records, not
formal unsatisfiability certificates); verified constructions for every n ≤ 30 give a(9) ≥ 13 and

the delivery README, which serves as the report README, still names a275672.tex and the
PDF. Nothing was submitted to the OEIS. Unrefereed; not formalized.

13 The Jacobian conjecture and Keller maps
Four reports continuing the formal project Algebra/JacobianConjecture, which verifies in Lean
and Rocq Alpöge’s three-variable counterexample and a degree-six stable representative in five
variables (its stabilization to all higher dimensions in Lean only); its further degree reductions are
SymPy certificates. None of the four reports’ own theorems is formalized; each README names
the project declarations it builds on. Three study the project’s three-variable map, one of them
its iteration; the fourth studies a different map, Gao’s five-dimensional F6 .
262. The fiber geometry of Gao’s Keller map F6
jacobian-conjecture/gao-f6-fiber-geometry/article.pdf
from ProveIt_Six_Sheets_Research.zip, ProveIt_Keller_Fibers_Research.zip
For Gao’s five-dimensional six-sheeted Keller map F6 (arXiv:2608.00222v1, §4.5.1), classifies every
geometric fiber: the cardinalities are exactly 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, all seven root-multiplicity patterns
occur, and off the hyperplane c = 0 the inverse points are the simple roots of an explicit sextic.
The omitted set is a smooth closed surface isomorphic to Gm × A1 , of codimension three, with the
single effective omitted parameter (21/32, 5/32, 25/128). Gives an explicit finite normalization
of rank six, the two-component nonproperness locus, the discriminant’s normalization and local
analytic types, global monodromy S6 with two arithmetic certificates, no proper intermediate
field, real fiber cardinalities {0, 1, 2, 3, 4}, and an obstruction to equivalence with a stabilization
of a map in at most three variables. The map is Gao’s; the fiber theory is the reports’. Merged
from two manuscripts proving the same core in identical normalizations; the second one’s different
proofs are kept as marked second routes. Not to be confused with the project’s formally verified
dimension-five stable map.
263. Arithmetic local–global dichotomies for the three-variable Keller map
jacobian-conjecture/arithmetic-local-global-fibers/article.pdf
from ProveIt_Arithmetic_Local_Global.zip; batch 58:      ProveIt_Dense_Integral_Hasse.zip; batch 64:
ProveIt_Beyond_Split_Fibers.zip
For the project’s three-variable Keller map, gives an explicit infinite family of integral targets with
a preimage in every Z3p but none in Z3 (each fiber is three rational points, so these are not rational
Hasse failures); classifies the split integer-root plane C = 2 by a gcd condition, with locally soluble
parameter density 27/(4π 2 ) surviving the removal of S-integral fibers; proves the exact integral
2-adic fiber law (21, 7, 3, 1)/32 for fiber sizes 0, 1, 2, 3, determined modulo 8; and contrasts it with a
rational Hasse principle over every number field. The complex stratification is credited to Gao and
the finite-field histograms to an external note. An intake remark records an integral triple collision
over the project’s own collision value. Not a new disproof of the Jacobian conjecture. Part II,
added in batch 58, answers Part I’s density question: such failures are Zariski dense in every plane
C = c ̸= 0, hence in A3 , and none lies on C = 0. It gives a complete local criterion for completely
split fibers, finds finitely many integral split fibers on each plane, and proves #Hcsp (T ) ∼ κ(c)T 2/3


                                                  105
for the completely split failures in square boxes |A|, |B| ≤ T , with κ(c) an explicit Gamma factor
times an Euler product. Part III, added in batch 64, gives a complete local-integral certificate for
every target, nonsplit fibers included, and on C = 2 counts the integral Hasse failures in the same
         27
boxes: 2π  2 T log T + O(T ), almost all of them nonsplit.

264. All-degree rigidity of a weighted Keller class
jacobian-conjecture/weighted-keller-rigidity/article.pdf
from ProveIt_Weighted_Keller_Rigidity.zip; batch 44:
ProveIt_Affine_Weighted_Keller_Classification.zip
For polynomial maps of weights (−1, 1, 2) written through t = xy and v = x2 z, classifies the Keller
maps in every degree over any field of characteristic zero: those with p, q affine in v and r affine
in (t, v) are an explicit tame family and a two-parameter diagonal orbit of the known map, so
every noninvertible member has coordinate degrees (7, 6, 4) and sixteen monomials, with no input
degree bound. Extends to r = R(t) + γv, where the possible maximum degrees of the noninvertible
members are exactly 7 and every even integer at least 8, and shows that with r = 1 + t + v the
fixed degree-seven coefficient scheme is the double point Q[ε]/(ε2 ), with an explicitly obstructed
tangent (a torus times it when the two coefficients of r are freed). The classification does not cover
unrestricted weighted maps. Sharpens to all degrees the project’s degree-seven sparsity search; it
does not show degree seven minimal in dimension three. Part II, added in batch 44, removes the
restriction on the coefficient of v in r: whenever p, q, r are affine in v that coefficient is constant,
so the classification covers the whole affine-in-z class; it adds dual-number slopes of every degree
and a universal second-order obstruction.
265. Dynamical degrees of the three-variable Keller map
jacobian-conjecture/keller-map-dynamical-degrees/article.pdf
from ProveIt_Keller_Dynamics.zip (batch 70)
Studies the project’s three-variable Keller map F under iteration, which neither the project
nor the other reports touch. Over any field of characteristic other than three, deg F n satisfies
dn+2√= 6dn+1 + dn with d0 = 1, d1 = 7 (1, 7, 43, 265, . . . ), so the first dynamical degree is
3 + 10; every positive weighted degree of every iterate is explicit, and every coordinate’s
downward-completed Newton polyhedron has exactly two vertices. Outside characteristic three,
composing √with a shear z 7→  
                                z + y 2 h(xy), h =
                                                 ̸ 0 of degree m, gives first dynamical degree
                2
 2m + 7 + 4m + 32m + 61 /2; in characteristic zero these maps are all Keller, noninjective, of
generic degree three and in one tame left–right equivalence class, yet their dynamical degrees
are unbounded. In characteristic three the second coordinate is y exactly and the degrees follow
separate closed formulas (first dynamical degree 4 or m + 3); in characteristic two the map is
purely inseparable of degree two. A monomial-dominance escape theorem gives a forward-invariant
complex escape region, and integral points there have maximal arithmetic degree and are Zariski
dense. No Lean or Rocq source is supplied; the formalization route is a plan.


14 Galois theory and radicals
Two reports continuing the formal project Algebra/PolynomialFormulas (Lean and Rocq root
formulas through degree four, Abel–Ruffini, and Lazard’s quintic solver); none of its theorems is
formalized.
266. Specialization-safe radical solvers
galois-theory-and-radicals/specialization-safe-radical-solvers/article.pdf
from ProveIt_Radical_Solvers_Research.zip; batch 52:    ProveIt_Optimal_Kummer_Atlases.zip
Gives an exact cyclotomic-resultant criterion for the characteristics in which equal-cardinality
subset sums of the roots of an irreducible prime-degree polynomial can collide, and evaluates it:
the bad characteristics are {5} for quintic pairs, {2, 7} for septic pairs and {2, 7, 29} for septic
triples. Classifies the collisions completely in characteristic 29 (exactly the cyclic polynomials
(X − η)7 − b) and in characteristic 2 (translated Z 7 + aZ 3 + bZ + d, realizing the nonsolvable

                                                 106
GL3 (F2 )). Constructs separating subset invariants in every characteristic, reconstructs all roots
from one coherent p-th radical on each Fourier chart once the intermediate-field invariant data are
given (generalizing Lazard’s pivot-one scheme for quintics; a coefficient-only solver is left as further
work), realizes every Fourier support by rational polynomials with full affine Galois group, and
shows the Picard group of the cyclic configuration quotient is Z/p, so no single global Kummer
coordinate exists. The Picard result has a written proof only. Part II, added in batch 52, answers
Part I’s question on the least number of charts: CH∗ (Conf p (Ad )/Cp ) = Z[t]/(pt, td(p−1) ), so every
cover by regular Kummer charts, even with nonlinear character sections and different characters
on different charts, needs exactly d(p − 1) charts (p − 1 for polynomials: four for quintics, six for
septics), and the Fourier charts attain it. This too is a written proof, by integral intersection
theory; its checker verifies only finite identities.
267. Finite separating ranges for sextic block resolvents
galois-theory-and-radicals/sextic-block-resolvent-separators/article.pdf
from ProveIt_Finite_Sextic_Separators.zip (batch 41); batch 57:
ProveIt_Three_Plus_One_Resolvents.zip; batch 70: ProveIt_Zero_Parameter_Sextics.zip
The project finds parameters separating the sextic pair and triple block-resolvent descriptors by
an unbounded Nat.find search. This report proves in writing that on the curve (t, 2t2 ) one of
t = 1, . . . , 196 always separates both families, sharply for arbitrary complex test sets, by orbit
counting. The bound is not formalized; formalizing it would make that search bounded, and two
further unbounded searches of the project remain. Part II, added in batch 57, shows that deciding
radical solvability of an irreducible sextic needs no separating parameter: the sextic is solvable
exactly when its triple resolvent has a base-field root at one parameter chosen in advance, or its
matching resolvent has one at each of three distinct parameters, because a base-field matching
root of a nonsolvable sextic comes from a Galois-invariant pentad, of which there is at most one,
and occurs at no more than two parameters. Part III, added in batch 70, sharpens this: one
compressed matching test at t = 0 together with one triple test decides, by an integer certificate
that the five values of a pentad are never all equal and by reciprocal transport; and since a
pentad’s points span an affine space of dimension at least two, the polynomial of false-positive
parameters has degree at most one and does not vanish at zero, so any two distinct matching
parameters suffice. It adds a denominator-free Pfaffian of order twenty for the constant triple
resolvent and a cubic 15 × 15 matrix polynomial whose determinant is the cube of the constant
matching resolvent.


15 Hilbert’s tenth problem
Twelve reports on Diophantine representations of computation that control their witnesses,
beside the formal project Computability/HilbertTenthProblem, which proves MRDP in both
directions in Lean, the universal pair (58, 4) and the single-fold unary exponential representation
of Jones and Matiyasevich. The first three (canonical certificates; probabilistic, quantum and
continuous computation; liveness beyond halting) continue that project. The other six, added in
batches 76, 78 and 79 (groups as substrates, signal machines, stochastic and thermal exactness,
quadratic orthant certificates, polynomial witness histories, smooth finalizers), treat further
substrates or witness domains and use MRDP at most as an imported theorem. Batch 79 also
extended five of the older reports. Batch 80 added Parts V and VI to the signal-machine report
and replaced the programs of three batch-79 Parts (canonical certificates Part XIX, quadratic
orthant certificates Part VI, signal machines Part IV) by their authors’ corrected code editions,
whose manuscripts are unchanged. Batch 82 opened five-particle-binary-automata and
fixed-universal-polynomials, the latter on the programme’s own fixed universal polynomial,
and extended the signal-machine report (Parts VI–VIII) and the group-substrate report (Part V);
batch 83 opened periodic-turmite-first-revisits and added Part XX to the canonical
certificates and Part V to the fixed universal polynomials; batch 88 added Parts II–IV to the
turmite report (a literal periodic Langton ant and a Diophantine certificate for the fixed machine

                                                  107
it simulates) and Parts VI–VIII to the fixed universal polynomials (the candidates below the
84-operation record). Batch 91 brought the pipeline’s Reports 49–71 (Report 68 was never
delivered): Part XXI of the canonical certificates (fixed-arity sandpile polynomials), Part VI of the
group-substrate report (a chronological matrix certificate), Parts IX–XIII of the signal-machine
report and Part VII of the five-particle report (smaller recognition radii); its two OEIS manuscripts
became a196460-clipping-tables. The last three entries below are batch 82’s and 83’s new
reports. None of the reports’ own theorems is formalized; each README names the project
declarations it builds on or relates to.

Diophantine representations that control their witnesses, not only their existence. For Petri nets
and interval-guarded translations, a convex quadratic integer polynomial whose natural zeros are
in bijection with the commutation classes of enabled executions of length T (its real zero set is a
rational polytope with exactly those lattice points), with quartic second routes, a Cartier–Foata
variant for bounded causal height, exact commutation tests, #P-completeness of counting and
an exponential lower bound for normal-form automata. Single-fold quartics for multisets and
nested repetitions of counter actions independent of the repetition counts, for “p(i) ≥ 0 for all
i ≤ T ” with O((d + 1)3 ) witnesses independent of T , for memory logs with 24L witnesses, and
for FRACTRAN and scheduled SKI runs. For cyclic-tag and deletion-tag systems and fixed-read
FIFO networks, certificates with one coordinate per consumed symbol and one guard slack, with no
intermediate queue quantified; faithful word memory with polynomial insertion at both ends needs
two natural coordinates. Proves that a single-fold (finite-fold) universal halting polynomial exists
exactly when every enumerable set has such a representation, an open problem not solved here.
Parts X–XIII, added in batch 62 from four manuscripts: quartic certificates for dynamic heaps
and local graph programs, with birth-order normalization of fresh names, two-stack universality
and a proof that perpetual memory safety has no existential representation; for polynomials
nonnegative on the real orthant, degrees two and three represent exactly the semilinear sets and
degree four every enumerable set, with single-fold quadratics for semilinear sets and order-free self-
assembly compilers; for first-enabled-rule multiset and FRACTRAN programs the sharp pumping
threshold 2H + 1, a uniform single-fold quartic and c.e.-completeness of eventual periodicity;
and conservative priority reaction networks with one shared fallback, with single-fold trace and
minimum-fuel quartics and a least-reservoir relation whose single-fold (finite-fold) representability
is again equivalent to that of every c.e. set. Part XIV (batch 63): one quartic per run-length
routing topology with exactly 4n + 4m + s witnesses and a unique root iff the run terminates,
and a fixed polynomial-time rank function whose single-fold (finite-fold) graph is equivalent to
the corresponding principle for all c.e. sets. Part XV, added in batch 78 from three manuscripts:
exact port wiring for Lafont’s interaction combinators. If M ̸= N are perfect matchings of 2n

                                                108
ports and Q is a uniformly random one, closing by Q gives different parities of cyclic wires with
probability between 2/5 and 2/3, both bounds sharp and the equality cases classified; bounded
six-rule compilers with O(C0 + F + T ) natural coordinates, degree four and one witness per legal
schedule (scheduled histories, not commutation classes), built on a minimal ten-state boundary
calculus, a ten-pattern annihilation kernel and memory certificates that are ledger variants of
Part V’s 24L (whose product lemma two of the manuscripts re-prove; no novelty is claimed for it);
and a canonical permutation-orbit certificate with an implemented fixed-schedule compiler. This
answers Part X’s question on an interaction-combinator frontend for bounded scheduled histories;
quotienting by independent schedules stays open. Part XVI (batch 78): for abelian sandpiles
on a finite undirected multigraph with a sink, an exact support criterion for the odometer and
cubic integer polynomials with exactly one natural zero for every input, carrying the odometer,
the stable endpoint and canonical burning ranks instead of a toppling history, with 10n + 6E
coordinates in the compact form (a directed counterexample fixes the scope); on Zd with a
stable periodic background, an exterior collar that certifies global halting exactly, with a unique
canonical radius that by Cairns’s imported universality theorem has no computable bound; and
the exact minimal eventual period of stabilization along an input ray. It answers Part XIV’s
question on infinite-background abelian computation for undirected graphs, and its single-fold
equivalence for Cairns’s relation is an instance of Part IX’s. Batch 79 added a second route inside
Part XVI, written independently: 12n + 16E quadratic residuals in 11n + 12E natural witnesses
with exactly one natural zero, carrying the odometer, whose sum of squares is a quartic with an
integral rejection gap, and on Z3 one fixed radius-one normal form with 47 finite-deviation fields
whose field solution exists, uniquely, exactly when the input stabilizes (neither ledger improves
the other). Part XVII (batch 79, from two manuscripts proving one theorem by two routes):
complete discrete sign charts of positive-base exponential polynomials along a loop, verified at
run endpoints only (a row of order m has at most 2m − 1 sign runs, sharp), with one witness
per input in arithmetic augmented by power atoms y = bn (O(D3 ) witnesses for fixed bases,
O(D4 ) with the bases as inputs) or, for an externally fixed bit bound, in an ordinary quartic; the
complete sign-chart graph of 2n − y has a single-fold (finite-fold) representationexactly when
every c.e. set has one, which sharpens a proposition of Part IX; and ℜ (3 + 4i)n changes sign
with positive density on every residue class. Part XVIII (batch 79): for reliable FIFO actions
along a straight-line trace grammar, a quartic with exactly 6g + 3c + 1 natural witnesses and
one zero exactly when the trace connects the supplied words, quantifying no intermediate queue;
exact macro repetition counts, infinite repeatability as a word equation, a Fine–Wilf pumping
threshold and unique-witness quartics for supplied infinite loops; its re-proofs of Parts I and VI in
one coordinate are printed as pointers. Part XIX (batch 79): for Barry Jay’s Tree Calculus under
eager evaluation and every externally fixed bound N on distinct calls, a quartic with 3N 2 + 19N
natural witnesses whose zeros project to the terminating applications (memoized proof DAGs,
made well-founded by additive call costs), a canonical refinement with exactly one witness, a strict
bracket-abstraction compiler from weak call-by-value λ-calculus, and a literal universal tree of 175
constructor-DAG nodes with an r.e.-complete halting domain, whose code of about 4.5 · 1010 bits
is charged by 175 quadratic equations and never materialized. Since batch 80 Part XIX ships its
authors’ corrected code edition, whose evaluator rejects non-exact or negative codes before any
state change, the repair proposed by the Hilbert’s-tenth research tree’s review; its manuscript is
unchanged. Part XX (batch 83, from two manuscripts, the pipeline’s Reports 35 and 36) delivers
the universal periodic input loader that Part XVI asks for: one explicit stable periodic background
on Z3 and a finite loader for every finite two-sided binary tape of the Neary–Woods machine
U15,2 , with finite total activity exactly when the machine halts, and finite binary prism certificates
whose natural zeros are the canonical odometers (a re-derivation of Part XVI’s certificate, marked
as such); replacing a product f g by f (g + k + c) makes every nonnegative real zero natural,
with the same summand count, degree, support and coefficient height, by an argument different
from the real gates of quadratic-orthant-certificates, which it credits. The research tree’s
review is printed with its findings, among them a real-algebraic limit on fixed-arity compression.

Dated notes of batch 88 record that Parts II–IV of periodic-turmite-first-revisits build

Part XXI (batch 91, the pipeline’s Reports 50 and 52–54) answers Part XX’s question on an

Merged from twenty-seven manuscripts; not a Lean formalization.

One fixed quartic represents the output law of every effective probabilistic or quantum program as
masses of projected roots; exact normalization budgets with a unique-witness quartic certificate;
normalized two-outcome probabilities are exactly the weakly computable reals, and conditional
comparisons are Σ02 -complete. Deciding whether a seven-outcome rational quantum instrument
in dimension four has an impossible outcome word is undecidable (via Neary’s matrix-mortality
theorem), with bijective quartics for words of fixed length. One rational piecewise-affine map of
the cube realizes every disjoint pair of enumerable sets as exact acceptance and robust rejection,
with quartic safety certificates for a computable critical-noise radius. Parts V–VII, added in
batch 62 from eight manuscripts: Boolean circuits compile to coercive integer quartics of height at
most 4 whose real zeros are Boolean, with uniform Hessian and rounding constants, so that halting
probabilities of fair-coin programs are root counts, component counts or Euler     √ characteristics;
birth–death chains with defect environments have the sharp spectral gap (3 − 2 2)/4 and a root
mass that is rational exactly for eventually periodic environments; unique-witness quartics for
coherent contraction trees over Z[eiπ/4 ], and dense rational SO(4) gate groups from the rotations
(3 ± 4i)/5, (3 ± 4j)/5 with c.e.-complete state transfer or c.e.-complete membership; a fixed
1/2-Lipschitz rational piecewise-linear map of R4 and a 1/2-contraction of Z2 with Σ01 -complete
exact half-space reachability and exact extinction; and unique-witness quadratics for rational
clipped neural networks, whose tied four-layer synthesis is equivalent to Hilbert’s tenth problem
over Q. Part VIII (batch 64): complete integer tube certificates, sums of squares of quadratics
with at most one witness, for reaching open rational targets under rational polynomial flows, and
global mass-action normal forms that carry them to chemical reachability. Part IX, added in
batch 76 from one manuscript: for time-homogeneous rational quantum loops (completely positive,

                                                110
trace-nonincreasing), the group inverse and Cesàro projection as the unique solution of four linear
equations with no rank choice, all stopping moments by one finite recurrence certified by a Faddeev–
LeVerrier circuit, unique seven-coordinate natural wires extending Part VI’s quartic compilation
to rational inputs, an exact weak-clock resolvent with an exponential limit law, a geometric
clock on the Eisert–Müller–Gogolin instruments giving every device the law (16/25)(9/25)n while
forbidden-record existence stays undecidable (also in dimension four, conditional on Part II), a
two-state counterexample to the unrestricted reading of Lardizabal’s rationality conjecture, and no
uniform existential Diophantine graph for computably scheduled one-dimensional loops. Its three
arguments repeated from Parts I, II and V are pointers. The report now points to the sibling reports
stochastic-and-thermal-exactness, which answers Part VII’s structural-subclass question
negatively in part (dated notes), and signal-machine-collision-certificates, which shares
no theorem. A dated note (batch 79) applies the finalizer of smooth-diophantine-finalizers
to Part V’s Boolean-circuit quartics, whose zero sets are singular at every real zero. Merged from
thirteen manuscripts; not a Lean formalization.
270. Beyond halting: recurrence, progress deadlines and Diophantine liveness
hilbert-tenth-problem/liveness-beyond-halting/article.pdf
from Beyond_Halting_Diophantine_Liveness.zip, Quadratic_Diophantine_Dynamics.zip (batch 62);
batch 63: quadratic_infinite_computation.zip; batch 79: clock_spectra_research.zip
For computable finitely branching systems, existence of a recurrent run with a computable progress
deadline is Σ03 -complete and equivalent to a nonempty effectively closed class of accepting schedules,
and to an accepting schedule of hyperimmune-free degree; fixed deadlines are Π01 , bounded gaps
Σ02 , unrestricted recurrence Σ11 -complete, with strict separations and every c.e. degree as an exact
deadline spectrum. An auxiliary-free degree-two polynomial with coefficients at most eight gives
the transition relation of counter networks (degree two is minimal); a second, incoming-edge
encoding has height six, a sum-of-squares quartic companion and an exact planar realization; and
a two-stack quartic certifies finite runs meeting prescribed deadlines. Part VI (batch 79) answers
in part the report’s question on deadline spectra beyond c.e. cones: clock problems are, up to
uniform computable transformations, the upward effectively closed mass problems of Baire space;
every ∆02 degree, and every degree of a unique path through a recursive tree (among them 0(r) and
0(ω) ), is a least clock degree of an input of a fixed two-stack system; unions of incomparable cones
have no least degree, and a least clock degree is hyperarithmetic and contains a self-modulus. Its
degree-two two-stack certificate is a second route, at lower degree, to the report’s quartic compiler.
Merged from four manuscripts; not a Lean formalization.

Low-degree certificates for computation in groups, all through the integral Heisenberg group H.

of canonical-diophantine-certificates; Remark 1.1 retains and refutes earlier sentences of

A rational signal machine computes by collisions of finitely many kinds of particles moving at
fixed rational speeds on a line. Once a complete finite collision history is fixed, its realizations are
exactly the natural zeros of a sum of squares of integer affine residuals, of degree two, with one
witness tuple each; typed endpoint conditions on maximal adjacency intervals exclude unrecorded


                                                  112
and omitted simultaneous collisions, and causal depth controls the denominators. Part I eliminates
every event coordinate: the realizing initial gaps form a relatively open rational polyhedral cone
cut out by at most 3(n − 1) + (3m + 1)E conditions, which gives small integral realizations, an
NP upper bound for bounded synthesis, a canonical first-hit quartic, exact quasi-polynomial
seed counts, an exactly solved first-collision tournament and a four-speed accumulating clock
with denominators 2 · 3k . Part II keeps event times and positions as witnesses on a rational
grid, adds halting, proves full column rank (strict convexity in the witnesses) and bounds the
canonical witnesses by 60K max(1, M0 ) for the four-speed interface of Durand-Lose’s universal
machine, which is not transcribed. Part I’s convex-quadratic barrier is a special case of Part XI of
canonical-diophantine-certificates, printed as a second route. No fixed-arity polynomial for
unbounded halting is claimed. Part III (batch 79) writes out a fixed reversible, number-conserving
universal signal machine with 114 meta-signals, 445 two-to-two rules and exactly 18 live signals
on every encoded input, compiled from Morita’s 15-state, 6-symbol reversible universal Turing
machine through Durand-Lose’s conservative stacks; its event dynamics is an 18-dimensional
partial piecewise homogeneous integer-linear map, and every fixed horizon has an explicit canonical
quadratic certificate (4,196,998 auxiliaries per step for the literal machine). This answers Part I’s
question on an audited universal frontend, except for an ordinary-integer loader; the manuscript
is the corrected edition of an earlier delivery, which is superseded. Part IV (batch 79) compiles a
fixed horizon of one-dimensional partitioned number-conserving lattice dynamics into one quartic
sum of squares with a unique natural witness, corrects a sign in Morita and Imai’s construction,
and proves decidability and effective semilinearity at total mass at most two; since batch 80
it ships its authors’ corrected code edition, whose polynomial terms must be exact integers (a
floating-point false zero is now rejected), the repair proposed by the Hilbert’s-tenth research tree’s
review. Parts V and VI (batch 80) find the least conserved mass of an undecidable observation in
Part IV’s weighted model. Part V: when many symbols have weight one, every separated reversible
two-counter machine compiles to a globally reversible, mass-conserving cellular automaton of
radius at most four (radius one by blocking) whose canonical simulation has total mass exactly
three; a universal source machine gives one fixed rule, so three is the least mass with undecidable
pattern occurrence there, and each fixed horizon has a degree-two certificate with a unique natural
witness. Its declared extension runs the source forward, then its inverse, then relabels, so that
exact-pair reachability is undecidable at mass three as well, with an exact first-target clock and a
compact certificate. Part VI: with at most one weight-one symbol the timed relation at mass at
most three is effectively Presburger, and exact reachability stays decidable at mass four, where
a binary radius-six shuttle with hit times k 2 + (2d − 11)k is not Presburger; so integer-state
number-conserving automata need at least five particles, and since batch 82 the binary five-particle
upper bound of five-particle-binary-automata makes five exact for binary rules. Batch 82
added a third source to Part VI: for a fixed input of mass at most four, disjoint half-open charts
and a quartic with exactly one natural witness tuple for each complete timed configuration
(four witnesses and seven squares for the binary shuttle, exact even over the nonnegative reals).
Part VII (batch 82, three manuscripts): with at most one weight-one symbol, exact and translated
reachability and pattern occurrence are decidable from every input of mass at most four on Zd ,
for every d and without reversibility (at d = 1 a second proof of Part VI’s theorem); semilinear
visited sets, eventually quasipolynomial box counts of sharp degree, quadratic clocks, the effect of
drift and canonical first-visit quartics; and a binary radius-six planar shuttle with exact wedge
and drift counts and a one-step quartic. Its sharp four-versus-five boundary in every dimension
is conditional on two Reports of the five-particle report. Part VIII (batch 82, two manuscripts):
for four fixed nonuniversal three-mass sources, emitted horizon-free polynomials (about 480 to
610 operations, exact degrees 2344 and 1192) with a positive zero exactly when the cleaned
automaton first reaches its exact target; every accepted fibre is infinite, and after the outer height
is made canonical the outer history is unique and projects bijectively onto an infinite native
fibre, a theorem implied by Part III of fixed-universal-polynomials; both rest on inherited
research-tree results. Batch 91 added Parts IX–XIII from fifteen manuscripts, the pipeline’s


                                                 113
Reports 49, 51, 56–67 and 69. Part IX: a binary number-conserving rule, globally reversible
with radius at most 90, whose four-particle orbit hits an anchored word exactly at the times
k 2 + (2d − 23)k, so four particles are the least for a non-eventually-periodic occurrence set under
reversibility too; and, conditional on Part VI’s sources, one finite family of timed charts for every
ordered input of mass at most four, with uniform stationary-frame Presburger reachability and a
single-fold quartic, while original-frame untimed reachability need not be Presburger. Part X: for
a supplied ultimately periodic complete macro with at most four live signals, infinite validity is
a finite formula of quadratic sign tests, compiled into a quartic with one natural witness tuple;
Zeno behaviour and rational deadlines are decided, and every valid such macro on rational data
has a rational accumulation time (Part I’s accumulation question in part); with five live signals a
half-contracted irrational rotation has a valid set that no finite sign formula describes, yet explicit
degree-twelve positive-integer certificates exist (81 witnesses and 44 equations for one machine).
Part XI realizes every invertible rational planar return, every fixed-centre projective lift and,
locally, every compatible invertible 3 × 3 homogeneous return with five live signals, and classifies
and certifies the strict iteration kernels. Part XII compiles every fixed two-counter program into
one number-preserving table with exactly five live signals and an exact nondestructive branch
test (Durand-Lose’s five-signal counters credited; no universality claim), pads every instruction
to exactly 30D and pays for native gap initialization inside two degree-twelve power modules.
Part XIII compresses the certificate of each fixed horizon to three, two or one positive witnesses,
shrinks the power module from 26 to 12 leaves, classifies when no witness is needed (restated with
proof in a196460-clipping-tables) and counts the encoded gap triples exactly; Report 68, which
it cites, was never delivered. Report 64’s strict bound 25D/18 < T < 25D/9 is attained at a = 1
and is corrected in a remark; the paid compiler for unbounded histories is absent, so no universal
operation, degree or witness bound changes. The research programme reviewed the archives before
placement and the written Parts after it (5b9101184, which corrected five summaries that could
be read as one scalar witness). The three-mass manuscript re-proves Part IV’s mass-two theorem
by the same argument (printed as a pointer), and the single-unit manuscript reruns Part IV’s
semilinearity architecture one mass higher (marked step by step). Four large packet and fixture
files of Parts III and IV, five rule and certificate exports of Part V (20.8 MB), the 70 emitted
circuit files of Part VIII and nine heavy evidence files of Part XIII (about 35 MB) are regenerated
rather than stored. Parts I and II are two independent manuscripts proving one theorem by two
routes, both printed in full; Parts III and IV come from two further manuscripts, and Parts V
and VI from four more, each Part a manuscript and a companion built on it (a fifth archive is

of Part VI, three for Part VII and two for Part VIII, and batch 91 fifteen more, two to four for

273. Stochastic and thermal exactness: exact events in uniformly convergent systems
hilbert-tenth-problem/stochastic-and-thermal-exactness/article.pdf
from batch 78: ProveIt_Exact_Erasure_Research.zip + ProveIt_Exact_Convergence_Research.zip +
Thermal_Arithmetic_Diophantine_Hamiltonians.zip; batch 79: Mixing_Does_Not_Remove_Arithmetic.zip,
Coercive_Green_Diophantine.zip + Well_Conditioned_Diophantine_Computation.zip
One pattern in five settings: an approximation converges uniformly at a known rate, while an
exact event of the same system is undecidable. Part I lifts integer matrix mortality to strictly
positive rational column-stochastic matrices with no common invariant complex line, entries in
any band around 1/n and total-variation contraction η T ; a product has rank one exactly when the
source product vanishes, so exact erasure is undecidable for at most eight positive 7 × 7 matrices
(from an imported mortality bound) and many-one complete for a nine-state family with a variable
alphabet, with horizon-T quartics having one witness per erasing word. Part II builds a fixed
turn-based stochastic game with discount 1/2, reward 1/4 and full-support dyadic rows: value
iteration converges at rate 2−n , yet exact attainment of the known fixed point 12 1 from a dyadic
terminal payoff is c.e.-complete; the one-player case stays open. Part III turns natural arithmetic


                                                 114
                                                 P
circuits into diagonal Hamiltonians H = (1 + i ni )Q of degree at most five whose zero states
biject with the witnesses; compact resolvent and finite thermal trace are equivalent to finite witness
multiplicity and undecidable on a padded universal family, and a uniformly computable critical
fugacity satisfies t∗ < 1 exactly on halting inputs. The quadratization, no-cutoff and finite-fold
steps are those of canonical-diophantine-certificates, printed as pointers; defects that the
research tree found in three shipped programs are disclosed, not patched, and Part II’s two largest
certificates are regenerated by its code rather than stored. Part IV (batch 79): every nonzero
rational polynomial P of translation rank r(P ) is realized by s = r(P ) + 1 states (optimal) with
commuting, invertible, strictly positive doubly stochastic rational matrices in any band around
1/s, acceptance 1/s + εq |n| P (n) and total-variation contraction θL ; on a fixed alphabet, with the
input on a rational quadratic Bézier curve or a line segment, the exact hit of the equilibrium value
is c.e.-complete, with no computable horizon. It reuses Part I’s lift, printed as a pointer. Part V
(batch 79, two manuscripts proving one theorem by two routes): uniformly coercive operators
αI − A on computable path forests, and on a connected graph of maximum degree three, with
polynomial-time approximable inverses, whose unique normalized integer Green certificates exist
exactly on halting inputs; the rational Green values are classified by a Pell equation, finite support
and attainment of a strongly convex energy are undecidable, and membership over Z[S −1 ] is
decidable for every finite prime set S. Merged from six manuscripts; Parts I–IV share no theorem;
a sibling of probabilistic-quantum-and-continuous-computation, not a Lean formalization.

outcome quadratics whose products are strong selector gates, so that every zero over the real


                                                 115
orthant is natural and every fibre is empty or a singleton. Part VI builds a unit-weight reset Petri

275. Polynomial witness histories: unique linear certificates with finite polynomial

from batch 79: unique_polynomial_histories.zip + one_coordinate_certificates.zip +

Certificates whose unknowns are a fixed number of finite polynomials rather than integers:
identities linear in them (in Part II of degree two, every product through one shared stride),
a clock that makes the whole witness unique with 0/1 coefficients, and no computable input-
only bound on its degree or support. Part I compiles cellular computation over N[X, Y ] with
s3 + s + 5 polynomial unknowns and 3s + 3 affine-linear rows (or 3⌈log2 s⌉ + 6 unit-coefficient

ring, a compiler with q + d linear identities in q + 2d sorted polynomial unknowns, a row-tagged

fixed number of polynomial unknowns is not a fixed number of integer unknowns: no ordinary
fixed-arity representation is claimed, the no-bound results are the bounded-search argument

one answering another; not a Lean formalization.

From six manuscripts, each reviewed by the programme without a defect (it certified only what it

set
probability/random-progressions-sharp-poisson/paper.pdf
from batch 138:   sharp_poisson_random_progressions.zip (arrival b28d0850b)
For the longest nonconstant arithmetic progression Un in a Bernoulli-p random subset of {1, . . . , n},
proves P (Un < r) = e−λ (1+cp λ2 r2 /n)+O((log n)3/2 /n) uniformly in r, with cp = (q/p)(5/3−π 2 /9)
(twice the integral of the squared binary entropy); the uncorrected error is exactly of order
(log n)2 /n, with a lattice-periodic coefficient and explicit limsup and liminf. No improvement of a
deterministic Szemerédi or van der Waerden bound is claimed. Placed here rather than at the
delivery’s path under Combinatorics/Ramsey/Research, whose directories refine deterministic
theorems formalized there; the delivered INTEGRATION.md, README and notes/ still name that
path. Unrefereed; not formalized.
